# Design register

**Owner:** Design Director (`main-2`) · **Opened:** 2026-07-30
**States:** Exploration → Candidate → Approved (Allen) → Implemented → Design-verified

One line per item. Built from the three slices' own packages, not from the charter's summary.
Paths are worktree-relative: `[ST]` = `surething-ui`, `[RM]` = `room-refinement`, `[TV]` = `tv-sweat`,
`[M2]` = `main-2`.

**Nothing in the studio is Design-verified yet.** No implementation has been through design review
under this seat. That is the gap this register exists to close.

---

| S1 | Direction — "The Annotated Form Guide" (world 4 of 7 candidates) | Approved · Allen 2026-07-28 | `[ST] docs/design/direction-concepts/DIRECTIONS.md` |
| S2 | Artboard + legibility laws — 1024×704, 13px product-fact floor, 4.5:1, 44×32 targets, no pure black, status never colour alone | Approved · Allen 2026-07-28 | `[ST] .../SHARED-SPEC.md` **S2-am + S2-am2 · batch 20:** the 13px floor stands but was expressed in canvas space against an output-space cost — no gate ever measured it where it lands. Right-sized after the close: NOT a per-item gate; ONE recorded baseline (ramp÷stroke at the acceptance view, on the PLAYER's path, instrument first characterized against a known kernel) so a future regression has something to regress from — the studio came one walkthrough from an illegible surface no gate could see. Magnification prescription retired (the bar is met); no type re-authored, not 'not yet' but not at all on this ruling **S2-am3-cl · batch 26: the legibility claim was an ARTIFACT of the contaminated frame — STRUCK; clause 2 stands, now CALIBRATED on harness frames; the baseline instrument amends to ramp over AUTHORED stroke** **Batch 29 (item 2): the fa93238 baseline STANDS** — floor unmoved across the flood removal (authored 1.115→1.152 / 1.243→1.246; measured flat). Ruled: the baseline shoot PINS ITS SLATE; per-frame boxes are the fallback, not the method — the recipe's own C34 pin-the-seed clause applied on its first live case (a record's x tracks the team name beside it). Bounded: deal-to-deal variation is an uncharacterised term inside these numbers — under C37 no movement of ~0.037 size may be called a change or a null on this baseline until a pinned-seed assertion exists. Recipe amendment routed to room **S2-am2-am3 · batch 30: the ~0.037 bound does NOT discharge on room's record and was never room's to discharge** — the pin ELIMINATES deal-to-deal variation rather than characterising it (stronger than asked, where it applies), but (1) the S2 boxes were never measured across the pair and ramp÷stroke is non-monotonic in blur, so no pixel delta carries a derivable bound into the ratio (C41 applied on the batch that wrote it); (2) decisive: fa93238 was shot BEFORE the pin existed — no property of today's rig reaches backwards. Bound passes to SureThing's pinned re-shoot with BOTH branches pre-committed before frames land: reproduces authored values under the asserted pin → fa93238 STANDS (now pinned), bound discharges; does not → fa93238 RETIRED, the re-shoot BECOMES the baseline, no reconciliation of old numbers attempted **S2-am2-am4 · batch 31 — BRANCH 2 CONFIRMED; the ~0.037 bound is REPLACED, not tightened.** Unit check first: the report printed BOTH forms labelled per group (C33-am3) — authored form recomputed: season records 1.127 vs 1.152 (moved 0.0247), row numbers 1.246 vs 1.246 (moved 0.0002). The lane's own Part A settles it: the ratio is only meaningful against an identical string at identical size — the season-record group does NOT satisfy that across deals, so 0.0247 is the instrument read OUTSIDE ITS OWN VALIDITY, not a noise term to bound. Standing structure: across-time regression baseline = ROW NUMBERS, authored form (deal-invariant, exact); within-frame legibility (clause 2) = both groups, measured form, one frame only. Two jobs, two groups, two forms — one number spanning all of it is why ~0.037 could not be right for any of it. The 0.025 stays an OBSERVATION at n=2 deals, never promoted. fa93238 RETIRED confirmed — for batch 30's reason (shot unpinned), not the movement's size. **DD seat error (§1.5): branch 1 was DEFECTIVE** — 'reproduces → stands, now pinned' would have retroactively pinned an unpinned frame; the branches disagreed about WHY, making them two guesses filed together; corrected form: reproduces → the pinned shoot becomes baseline anyway. Cost nothing by luck, not design. Phone baseline (0.567, PHONEREF01/msgs-03) noted deal-dependent with NO invariant subgroup — never compared across time at another seed. Part A endorsed as the C37 characterization this instrument never had |

| S73 | Presenting a same-game ticket's price honestly, against a board of multipliable odds | **RULED** · DD 2026-08-12 batch 45, on the SGP lane's step-1 recommendation (`docs/sgp/`, `bbd67e6`). **The lane re-put the question correctly** — correct pricing LENGTHENS 78.07% of two-leg tickets, so the problem is not shortening but **INCONSISTENCY**: the shapes players most want to build are the ones that shorten (~32% at three legs, ρ median 1.63) while a corners-and-cards ticket lengthens, so no stable rule forms and the only available intuition (*related legs pay less*) is wrong for most tickets. **The diagnosis: TWO EFFECTS ARE SUPERIMPOSED and the surface shows only their sum** — (1) **correlation**, where related legs add less risk than they appear to and the ticket shortens, and (2) **vig**, where the naive product charges margin once per leg while the joint charges it once, lengthening everything. **The player cannot learn the rule because he sees one number containing both, and only one of the two is something he can act on.** **RULED, seven clauses.** **(1) A same-game ticket is its OWN INSTRUMENT, never a parlay with an adjustment — the surface NEVER displays a product-of-legs figure for it, and never an adjustment, a correlation discount, a was/now, or any deduction line.** This does the most work, so the reasoning is on the record: **the nerf reading is manufactured by the comparison and the comparison is manufactured by showing the product** — a surface printing *1.85 × 2.10 × 3.40 = 13.21, adjustment −32%, price 8.98* has **literally rendered a number being taken away from the player**, and no copy fixes that. There is nothing to deduct from if nothing is presented to deduct from; a same-game ticket is one bet on one compound outcome and it has A PRICE. **(2) The relationship is MARKED, in the house's own ink, which already means this:** oxide is *the house acting on the document* (S3, S15's strike its worked case) and biro is *what the player chose* — **the player picks his legs in biro, the house marks the connection between them in oxide.** Nothing is invented; this is the existing ink law doing the job it was written for, and it is the identity of the surface — **a correlation IS an annotation**, and the machine noticing that two legs tell one story is the most in-character thing this product could do with the fact. It also solves learnability with no formula: the rule becomes **"legs the house has marked pay less"**, which is true, visible and actionable. **(3) The LENGTHENING is not remarked** — no badge, no *better value*, no flag; a product congratulating itself for charging less is exhortation in the register T27 and S45 both closed. Silence is not concealment, because per clause 1 nothing ever claimed the price was a product. **(4) THE DIAL CONSTRAINT ON STEP 2, which binds pricing rather than presentation: any ticket priced materially shorter than the naive product must carry a relationship the surface can NAME.** Shortening a ticket whose relatedness cannot be marked charges the player for something he cannot see at the point of spending — **S17's class exactly**. **So the correlation model must expose a NAMEABLE RELATION, not only a scalar ρ**; the dial stays free in magnitude and is **constrained in kind**. Two costs to carry now rather than discover at step 5: the model emits a **relation label** beside its joint probability (enough to compose a sentence, not enough to be a formula), and **where it finds correlation it cannot label, the price does not move** — a deliberate constraint on pricing. **(5) Impossible combinations are refused at the slip, in words, once** — **not a disabled control** (S24 bans the disabled state outright; S56 bans a distinction in a channel the player cannot see); the leg stays reachable alone so **C19 is untouched** (the engine prices the leg, it refuses to price the COMBINATION); the refusal states the fact in the house's ink — *these two cannot both land*. **A bet that cannot win must never be purchasable:** a price is a factual claim about an outcome, and selling a finite price on an impossible event is the product lying in the one place it promised not to. **(6) An implication leg SAYS SO** — for the 22 implication shapes the second leg moves the price by roughly the margin alone, and **a leg that lands and changes nothing reads as broken and teaches a false rule**; the surface states it in the machine's voice (*this adds nothing; the first leg already contains it*), which is the same annotation channel as clause 2 and the most characterful moment in the feature. **(7) No formulae on the face** — T21's principle: values appear when the sim emits them as first-class, never computed in presentation; no ρ, no multiplier, no percentage is ever printed. **COUPLING TO ALLEN'S CALL #1, named because the lane's four questions are not independent: clause 2 is what destroys the exploit as a discoverable mechanic**, since preserving it would require the surface to HIDE the connection and clause 4's problem then returns with no remedy. **The design side of that decision, which the lane did not make: the exploit exists only under INCORRECT pricing, and incorrect pricing here means selling tickets that CANNOT WIN at finite odds up to a mean decimal of 2070.70.** That is not a discoverable mechanic — a price is a fact, and **S45 is the standing law that satire never occupies a slot where a fact belongs**. Recommended to Allen: price honestly, move the exploit to relics, **where a relic breaking a book the player has learned to trust is worth far more than a bug found in a book that was never correct**. **FLAGGED NOT RULED — the instrument needs a name:** if it is its own instrument it needs its own word and **`SGP` is industry jargon** against S22's vocabulary law; `SAME MATCH` is the obvious direction and is a fact rather than a brand — **material naming is Allen's** (S16, S46 both his), raised now so step 2 does not bake `SGP` into player-facing strings. **TV CONSEQUENCE noted, not ruled:** a same-game ticket's legs appear in the TV ticket column during the sweat and **the mark must survive that trip or the two surfaces disagree about what the ticket is**; TV's leg rows are ruled at T24/T40/G1 with its own ink register, so how the mark renders there is TV's question — scheduled rather than discovered, and it does not block step 2 **S73-am · batch 46 — Q1/Q2/Q3 ruled on the lane's sharper brief, DRAWS-AWARE; batch 45's DIAGNOSIS is superseded and every ruling in it survives on stronger ground.** **The diagnosis first:** batch 45 built the learnability problem on *two superimposed effects* — correlation shortening, **vig lengthening everything** — which was correct for the state in `step-1-recommendation.md` (78.07% lengthen at `Ω_sgp = Ω`). **The ADOPTED margin rule removes the vig half:** `shown price ÷ product = 1/ρ`, so **51.4% of two-leg tickets are exactly independent and price IDENTICALLY to the multiplied number.** Recorded as a **moving input, not a misreading** — the dial was set after the document ruled on — and the consequence is that **the divergence is no longer a mixture but a clean signal appearing ONLY where correlation exists**, which is the condition clause 4 was written to require. **One thing gets harder and is named: intermittent correctness teaches a habit more strongly than uniform divergence** — the player multiplies, is **exactly right 51.4% of the time**, then is silently wrong by ~39% on the shapes he most wants to build; a rule that works half the time is learned as a rule that works. **Q1 — RULED: the qualitative marker, option 3, no arithmetic.** Not *show nothing* (the brief makes the case itself — a player who multiplies and finds a discrepancy has found a REAL one and deserves an answer; silence is **S17's class**). Not *show both numbers* — it makes the slip a calculator (**T21**), renders the same fact twice (**S58**), and puts a number visibly moving on the document; it would not always be a deduction, since negatively correlated tickets pay MORE, but a two-directional correction line is **worse**, teaching the player to watch a number change rather than read his ticket. **What the mark buys is the whole argument: the player's complete and CORRECT mental model needs no arithmetic — *unmarked legs multiply; marked legs pay less*.** The mark is what **makes** the multiplication rule work rather than what replaces it, telling him exactly when his own arithmetic applies. **The fairness fact is NOT printed:** the brief offers that a same-game ticket carries the same house edge as an equivalent cross-game parlay and that this is defensible — it is true, and it stays off the slip, because **a house that explains its own fairness at the point of spending reads as a house with something to explain** (S44's boundary — the machine does not wear the house's brand, and the house does not testify about itself on the player's document). **Q2 — RULED: WARN. Not block, not silence.** **Silence refused outright** — the brief's own *"quietly milked by a trap they cannot see"* is **S17 verbatim**, and it is the most corrosive thing available to a product whose promise is that the numbers do not lie. **Blocking refused, and NOT on paternalism, which is the weaker argument: a redundant leg is a LEGAL bet, correctly priced.** This game is about a player learning to read a book — **a house that prevents you from being stupid is not this product; a house that tells you and lets you do it anyway is exactly this product** — and blocking forecloses uses nobody has anticipated (a relic, a challenge condition). **The clutter objection dissolves:** the warning appears on **22 shapes**, not on every slip. Copy states the fact naming both legs — *`OVER 1.5 GOALS adds nothing — OVER 2.5 GOALS already contains it`* — **and it is the most credible thing in the feature because it costs the house money to say**, which is what makes the rest of the machine believable. **Q3 — RULED DRAWS-AWARE; the trap does not partially dissolve, it GOES.** Allen greenlit draws today, and **both artefact shapes were impossible only because 1–1 was unrepresentable** — with draws, `BTTS YES + Under 2.5` is a legal, ordinary, popular ticket; not refused, no copy written, nothing left to expose. **STANDING RULE, bigger than the two shapes: the surface refuses only what is impossible in FOOTBALL, never what is merely impossible in the MODEL; where the model is more restrictive than the game it simulates, the MODEL is the defect and the refusal does not ship.** **Why it is a law and not a copy preference: a refusal is the machine making a factual claim about the world**, and if it says *these cannot both happen* about something real football does every weekend, a football-literate player catches the product lying — **and every other number the machine shows loses its credit at the same moment. This surface's authority is that it does not lie about facts, and that authority is indivisible.** It generalises past draws, since any v1 simplification manufactures the same false refusals: **before a refusal ships it is checked against football, not against the model**, and the check states what it could not cover (C18 §4.2). **STEP-3 CONSEQUENCE, the build reach the brief predicted: the impossible set is RE-DERIVED after draws land** — the 22 two-leg and 57 three-leg shapes were enumerated against a drawless model and that enumeration is now stale **in the direction that matters, over-refusing**; every shape re-checked, the two known artefacts expected to leave. **The refusal reads:** *`OVER 2.5 GOALS and UNDER 1.5 GOALS cannot both land`* — the fact, both legs named, once, at the slip in its own space; **not a modal** (S55), **not a disabled control** (S24, S56), no scolding (S71/S72 — name the state, not the owner). It teaches because it is **specific**: the player learns which two legs conflict, which is real football knowledge he keeps. **THE INK SPLIT, stated because three annotations now exist and will otherwise be muddled: OXIDE for the correlation link** (the house's mark ON the player's picks — S3's own definition, the dead-leg strike's family); **TONER for both statements** — *adds nothing* and *cannot both land* — (the machine stating a fact in its own space, S26's *state rather than exhort*); **BIRO for none of them, ever**, biro being only what the player chose, with S26 the worked case of getting exactly this wrong. The distinction that keeps oxide from diluting: **oxide marks the house acting on his picks; toner is the machine saying something true — a refusal is not the house acting, it is arithmetic, spoken** **S73-am2 · batch 47 — ALLEN'S DECISION: THE HONEST BOOK IS YES.** The coupling named at S73 resolves in the direction recommended — **the mark ships, the exploit dies.** Clause 2 is unblocked and step 2 proceeds under clause 4's constraint: the model emits a **relation label** beside its joint, and **where it cannot label a correlation the price does not move.** **Consequence ROUTED, not executed:** `02-betting-math.md:24` carries the *"correlated parlay as discoverable exploit"* framing and that framing is now dead — it is a **shared canonical document, edited at integration and not by this seat** (STUDIO.md's ownership rule), so it is named as an owed edit rather than made; the replacement is on the record at S73, **the exploit moves to relics, where a relic breaking a book the player has learned to trust is worth more than a bug found in a book that was never correct.** **STILL ALLEN'S AND STILL OPEN: the mark's name**, held for him as is the instrument's (`SGP` is jargon against S22; `SAME MATCH` offered as the direction) — **named here so step 2 bakes neither into player-facing strings**, and the build can proceed on placeholders since neither name changes a mechanism **S73-am3 · batch 48 — ALLEN'S TWO NAMES, INTO CANON: the instrument is `SAME MATCH`; the mark is `THE HOUSE'S LINE`. Player-facing strings may be authored REAL from this line — no placeholders on either.** **Why the mark's name is better than the brief that asked for it, recorded because the lane builds against it: `THE HOUSE'S LINE` is true in BOTH registers at once** — it is *literally* a line the house draws on the document connecting two of the player's picks, and *"the line"* is already the betting word for what the book says. **The name is simultaneously the physical mark and the house's position, which is exactly what the mark is** — the annotated-form-guide identity in three words, arriving with nothing to explain. It sits correctly against **S3** (oxide is *the house's mark*): the mark now has a name that says whose it is, in the surface's vocabulary rather than the industry's. **RULED, because recording a name without ruling where it appears leaves the lane to guess and the wrong guess is specific and likely — `THE HOUSE'S LINE` printed beside the mark on every slip. THE MARK IS DRAWN, NOT CAPTIONED.** That would be **the house captioning its own annotation** — the move already refused at Q1 for the fairness fact, and **S44's boundary again: the machine does not narrate the house's presence on the player's document**; a mark needing a label on every occurrence is also a mark that is not working. **(1) The oxide line is drawn and carries NO LABEL** — it connects the picks and says what it says. **(2) Where a statement is needed it states the RELATION, not the name** — S73 clause 4 already requires the model to emit a relation label *"enough to compose a sentence"*, and this is that sentence: **what the legs SHARE**, in toner, in the machine's voice, once per slip, never announcing the apparatus. **(3) `THE HOUSE'S LINE` is what the thing is CALLED** — used where the product refers to it (rules copy, the ledger, a first-encounter explanation) and **never as a per-occurrence caption.** **The onboarding gap is real and is answered by the STATEMENT, not the name:** the first time a player sees an oxide line he cannot decode it (S56's class), and **the toner sentence is what teaches it, on the slip where it happens, in the same breath as the mark** — the name is how he refers to it afterwards. **`SAME MATCH` form:** uppercase as the surface's market vocabulary already is (`OVER 2.5 GOALS`, `MUSKRATS ML`), **a role printed as a word** per S22's own requirement, a fact rather than a brand; tracking follows the laptop's label treatment under `surething-design.md` §4.3 with **the token the kit's to supply, not this seat's to invent** (S70's endorsed precedent — leave it untracked rather than guess). **Nothing else moves: both names are VOCABULARY, and neither changes a mechanism, a price, a refusal, or a mark's ink** — S73 and S73-am stand exactly as ruled **S73-am4 · batch 55 — THE OWNING-DOC FOLD SURFACED A CONFLICT AND THE OWNING DOC WINS (§1.2): the impossibility refusal takes STAMP, not toner, and must carry CAUSE AND REMEDY.** Batch 46's ink split put both statements in toner on the reasoning that *a refusal is not the house acting, it is arithmetic spoken*. **`surething-design.md` §3.3 already rules the state**: **Blocked = muted label + STAMPED literal reason ≥13px, CAUSE AND REMEDY, inside the control** — and **a refused leg combination IS a Blocked state**, so it takes the treatment the surface already has rather than a new one. **Two corrections, both this seat's: (1) the ink** — Stamp, not toner, because the house declining to take a bet is the house acting on the document, which is Stamp's own definition; **(2) the copy** — batch 46's `X and Y cannot both land` gives the CAUSE only, where this row has always required **cause AND remedy**, so the refusal also names what to drop. **Constitution §1.2 decides it without argument** — the owning document outranks the register's ruling for that item on that surface's content — and **§6's *a clause that contradicts an owning document is a conflict to escalate, not a licence* is why this surfaced at the fold rather than in a build.** **UNCHANGED and re-confirmed against the doc: the implication warning is NOT a Blocked state and does NOT take Stamp** — a legal-but-pointless leg is legal, correctly priced and added, and the machine states the fact **in toner, in its own space**; blocking it stays refused (*a house that prevents him from being stupid is not this product*), and silence stays refused as S17's invisible cost. **`THE HOUSE'S LINE` sits correctly as a named use of Stamp** — §3.1's third row doing the job it was written for — **drawn, never captioned.** **The value of folding, recorded: a ruling that never meets the owning document never meets its own surface's existing law.** | batch 45 |
| S74 | The draw on the board, and the forms it needs | **RULED** · DD 2026-08-12 batch 49, on the draws work (`markets-pregame` D1, `3dc7a03` — *DRAWS. The moneyline is 1X2*). **The engine fact that decides it: `MatchResult {Home, Draw, Away}` while `Side` STAYS TWO-VALUED as a TEAM designator, and `Pick.Side`/`Leg.Side` now THROW on Draw. The draw is NOT a team** — the engine ruled that structurally and **the surface must not undo it in presentation**; a draw rendered as a third team is a category error the code has already refused. **S24 predicted this exact moment** (*paired pricing describes two-outcome markets; scorer renders as a single-column offer list, never a paired row with a dead cell*): **the moneyline is no longer a two-outcome market**, so the paired row no longer describes it and must not be forced to. **RULED: three offers, the draw in the MIDDLE** — its position is not borrowed convention but **meaning**: the draw sits between the two teams because it is the outcome where **neither wins**, and putting it at either end would make it look like a third competitor, which is exactly what `Side` refuses to let it be. **Named as what it is — `DRAW` — never as a team and never with a team's treatment: no pitch dot** (T2 gives muted blue and pink to the two sides; a draw has no side and takes no hue), no crest, nothing team-coloured. **`1X2` NEVER REACHES THE PLAYER** — industry jargon exactly as `SGP` was, and **S22 governs**: the surface composes and the role is printed as a word; `1X2` is a code word for a market shape and stays in the code. **G1's DECK OWES A DRAW FORM AND THIS IS THAT AUTHORING** — G1's scope line said *a seventh market needs a form authored before it ships*, and while the draw is not a seventh market but a **third outcome on an existing one**, the moneyline's authored forms **assume a team** (`MIDDLEMEN ML`) and a draw leg has none. **The word already exists on this surface: T62 records the live leg reading `LEVEL 0–0 at 14`, so `LEVEL` is already the product's word for a tied scoreline** — nothing is invented. **NEED** (requirement, while live) = `LEVEL AT FULL TIME`; **progress** (state, beneath) = `LEVEL` / `NOT LEVEL`; **compact** (identity elsewhere) = `DRAW`. **T70's pair check passes by T70-am's own test rather than by luck** — the check is an INFORMATION test, not word-overlap, and *shared words carrying different facts are legal*: `LEVEL AT FULL TIME` is a requirement at full time, `LEVEL` is the state right now. **Fit is NOT asserted** — these are new strings in the canon face, they measure against their columns like everything else, they join the sweep's population under C46, and any overrun routes to T74 with the rest **S74-am · batch 65 — THE BOARD'S DRAW ROW: THE COMPOSITION, ruled from the board's own grammar.** S74 ruled the FORM and the owning document carries it, but **neither carries the LAYOUT** — §"The draw on the board" states only the negative (*a two-outcome paired row no longer describes this market*), **and a negative is not a composition.** **THE BOARD'S GRAMMAR, READ OFF THE FRAME rather than assumed** (`phase-l-push/final/…-01-form-lobby-flat-1024x704.png`): **a matchup is NOT one row with two price cells — it is TWO STACKED LINES, each carrying one side, with the price on the same line as the team it belongs to** (`NOTARIES 4-5 · AWAY −156` above `FERRETS 5-4 · HOME +127`, `MORE ›` spanning both). **Two facts in that layout decide everything: (1) THE PRICE CELL ALREADY CARRIES THE OUTCOME WORD, NOT THE TEAM** — it reads `AWAY −156`, not `NOTARIES −156`; the left column names WHO and the price cell names WHICH OUTCOME, **so the board already has the draw's grammatical slot and nothing is invented**, the same shape as S74's own finding that `LEVEL` already existed; **(2) the organising principle is *the price sits beside its subject*, which is what makes the slate scannable and the thing a third offer can most easily break.** **RULED — THE DRAW TAKES ITS OWN LINE, BETWEEN THE TWO TEAMS:** `AWAY −156` / **`DRAW +240`** / `HOME +127`, with **`DRAW` in the PRICE CELL exactly where `AWAY` and `HOME` live** — never in the matchup column, which names teams and where `DRAW` is the category error S74 and the engine both refuse. **The matchup column is EMPTY on that line and EMPTY IS THE CORRECT RENDERING OF "NEITHER" — this is NOT S24's dead cell**, S24 having refused *an offer slot with no offer* while here the **SUBJECT** slot has no subject, **and naming anything there would invent the third competitor `Side` exists to refuse.** **The middle position becomes LITERAL** — S74 ruled it is meaning rather than borrowed convention, and **in this layout it is meaning you can SEE**: the draw's line sits physically between the two teams', attached to neither. `MORE ›` spans the block, now three lines. **No team treatment on that line** — no dot, no crest, no hue (T2 gives the two sides muted blue and pink; **a draw has no side and takes no hue**). **THE COST, NAMED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED: a third line per block is roughly a third fewer matchups visible** — line pitch 38px, block pitch 78px, list area ~490px, so **six blocks today and about four at three lines** *(eyeball readings off the rendered canvas at this seat, not measurements — the lead measures them)*. **This does NOT breach C19:** the interior market list **scrolls** (S25-am) with **S27's printed position rail**, so every priced offer stays reachable by a mechanism that already exists. **§2's yield order is NOT invoked and must not be** — **nothing here is a deficit to yield against**: a third outcome is a product fact ARRIVING, not a layout overflowing, and §2's own sentence binds — *nothing that states a product fact is deleted to make a layout fit.* **`MONEYLINE` STANDS as the column header** — it names the MARKET, not the number of outcomes, and the market's identity has not changed; **renaming it would be a vocabulary change with no defect behind it** (S22 governs; `1X2` remains out). **OWED: the block's re-derived height and visible-count, MEASURED** — re-deriving a fixed grid constant **once at design time is explicitly legal** (§2, T51, S40) while **a zone resizing to content at runtime is not.** **PRE-COMMITTED: (1) every match prices a draw** — `MatchResult {Home, Draw, Away}` says it does — **→ every block is three lines, uniform, closes with no further ruling; (2) some matches price no draw → THE BLOCK IS STILL THREE LINES** with the draw's line empty, **because a ragged board whose block height depends on the market IS a zone resizing to content**, which §2 forbids, and an empty line is honest where a collapsing block is not; **(3) on frames the blank matchup column makes the draw's line read as detached or as a separator → the remedy is the PRICE CELL's own treatment**, which already carries the word, **never a token in the matchup column.** **Fit is not asserted** — new strings in the canon face, measured against their cells, joining the sweep's population under C46 | batch 49 |

The spec's job was to decide WHICH half he keeps in view, and it keeps the half he spends from** | batch 78 **SIZED ON A'S SPLIT — `S83-am`, batch 79.** A's 10.00px lands in **three different zones, which is why the per-block report was asked for**: **−4.00 header gap → zone 1 (head 44 → 40)** · **−4.00 the bare post-leg gap → zone 2 (content −4)** · **−2.00 payout label → zone 3 (reserve 318 → 316)**. **So `530 − 40 − 316 = a 174px viewport` (was 168) against a worst content of 238 — a ~64px scroll, and the EXACT figure comes from the factored measurement (~60.10 for the same state), not from this arithmetic; the 4px is the cursor-sum-against-measured-bounds established at batch 74 and the factored number governs.** **AND THE MARGINAL-SCROLL HAZARD C WAS SEQUENCED TO AVOID IS MEASURABLY GONE:** post-A in zone 2, **4 legs alone = 168 against 174 — FITS, no scroll at all**; +a held consumable 202 (scrolls 28); +the statement 204 (scrolls 30); +both 238 (scrolls 64). **The ordinary composition does not scroll and the scroll engages only where a consumable is held or a sentence is present** — precisely the property batch 78 asked A to deliver, now measured rather than hoped for. **C's sequencing condition is DISCHARGED and C is clear to build** **· `S83-am2` batch 80 — THE TWO SEAMS, and this corrects this seat's own spec.** Batch 78 ruled three zones and their contents and **never ruled what happens WHERE THEY MEET** — with zone 2 scrolling between two fixed neighbours there are now two seams that did not exist, and **content sliding under an opaque block with nothing between them reads as a COLLISION, not as a scroll.** **THE COMMIT SEAM (zone 2 → 3) TAKES 6px, AND T47 IS THE DERIVATION:** T47 exists for exactly this problem one boundary down — *the flow region and the anchored band must never meet* — **but under C its `+ 6f` has become an INTERNAL gap inside zone 3, between the payout and PLACE. The seam it was ruled to protect moved up and nothing moved with it.** **RULED: 6px of ground at the zone 2/3 seam, on T47's own reasoning, and it is as true when the flow scrolls as when it does not — more so, because the content is moving.** **And the seam is only meaningful if content stops at it: zone 2 CLIPS at its viewport (`RectMask2D`) and never draws into the 6px or under zone 3 — a pad behind an opaque block is not a pad, it is a hidden overlap.** **THE HEAD SEAM (zone 1 → 2) TAKES NOTHING, and the board settles it:** `BoardTitle` is a fixed column head with `titleStripHeight = 26f` for a 26px box and **`BoardBody` clips immediately beneath with no separating pad**, the arrangement Allen ruled permanent and this seat measured. **A column head and a dense control block are not the same kind of neighbour — one line of muted text at the fact floor does not need defending from the rows beneath it; a stack of five controls carrying the money does.** **So A's `8 → 4` header harvest is NOT disturbed** *(this seat expected the opposite and checked before writing: a moving boundary looked like it needed more separation, and the board's own arrangement says what matters is what sits BELOW the seam, not whether the content moves)*. **VIEWPORT CORRECTED TO 168, superseding batch 79's 174**: `530 − head 40 − reserve 316 − seam 6`. **CONSEQUENCE, and it looks like bad news and is not: 4 legs alone = 168, EXACTLY FLUSH, still no scroll** — batch 78's sequencing condition holds — **and the scroll distances are 34, 36 and 70, exactly the two bills and their sum. The arithmetic closes on itself: the scroll IS the overrun, neither more nor less, which is what shows the zone split neither created nor absorbed a pixel.** **Flush is named as tight: 168 into 168 has no clearance, so batch 78's 1px dead-band is load-bearing rather than defensive, and the gate must exercise the 4-legs-alone state explicitly — a state sitting exactly on its boundary is the one worth pinning** **· BUILT — CODE GRANTED, DESIGN-VERIFICATION OWED · batch 81** (`0dbdd62`). **Every batch-78/80 clause verified against source at this seat**: three zones built · **T47 extended by construction** (every commit constant builds UP from `ActionBandReservedHeight`, and the band's drop reuses the kit derivation S51 closed on rather than restating it) · **THE HOUSE'S LINE parented to the scroll body with its rows** (`DrawHouseLine(flow, …)`) · rests at top · clips via `RectMask2D` · head seam flush as ruled · zones asserted to sum at 0.001px · boundary state pinned non-scrolling · **and every commit control — `StakeLabel`, `Stake`, `PayoutLabel`, `Payout`, `Place` — asserted to have NO `ScrollRect` ancestor.** **Re-derived here to the tenth: 160 → +36.1 → +16 → +32 → +34 → +34 = `CommitZoneReserved 312.1`, `SlipViewportHeight = 177.9`.** **The scroll distances 24.1 / 26.1 / 60.1 are the old overruns exactly — batch 78's invariance holding on the built thing, which is how we know the split neither created nor absorbed a pixel — and 14 of 20 states clear without scrolling.** **THIS SEAT'S BATCH-80 ARITHMETIC WAS WRONG AND THE BUILD'S CORRECTION IS ACCEPTED IN ITS CONCLUSION** (four legs alone CLEARS rather than sitting flush, so the 1px dead-band is NOT load-bearing) **but its diagnosis is not: A was already in my number — the 3.9px error is that I costed the payout block at its CURSOR ADVANCE of 40 where the reservation uses the kit's derivation of 36.1. That is S51's 3.9px, established by this seat at batch 74 and not applied to its own arithmetic one batch later. A seat that derives a constant and then reasons with a different one has two sources for one quantity — the defect it spent the same morning ruling against, and its own spec said the viewport comes from the factored measurement rather than this arithmetic.** **THE READ IS NOT GRANTED: there is no capture.** The merge adds `SportsbookApp.cs` and `SureThingEntryTests.cs` and nothing else — **everything granted above is a reading of source, none of it a reading of the screen.** **`S83` is BUILT; `S83` is NOT Design-verified** (C11). **Capture ordered, with T99's binding condition: shoot the state that SCROLLS — four legs plus a held consumable, scrolling 24.1px — because a capture of a non-scrolling state proves nothing about a scroll; the boundary state may ride as the comparable, not as the evidence.** **AND THE GESTURE IS NOT BLOCKED BY THIS: it waited on the reservation being PROVISIONED, which it is. Unblocking the gesture and Design-verifying C are DIFFERENT THINGS — the first is granted, the second is owed a capture — said explicitly because a row that is built, granted on code and awaiting frames is exactly the state that quietly becomes "done" if nobody writes down which half is outstanding** |

| S83-am3 | The seam clause, amended by what the build revealed — rects were the wrong object | **AMENDED; the 6px reserve WITHDRAWN as the mechanism, its reason kept, a gate replacing it** · DD 2026-08-15 batch 81. **The 6px is not built — zone 2's viewport bottom and zone 3's top are both −217.9, exactly flush. By the LETTER of batch 80 that is a miss; by its REASON it is close to correct, and the gap between those two is my clause being written about the wrong thing.** **T47's band meets its neighbour AS INK — `PLACE` is a filled control occupying its rect to the edge, so rect-flush there IS ink-flush and 6px of reserved ground is the only separation available.** **Zone 3's top element is the STAKE row and it does not: label and figure sit in 30px boxes inside a 34px advance, `LowerLeft`/`LowerRight` with baseline alignment (M-05), so the ink starts well below the rect's top edge and a clipped leg row does not abut a glyph.** **The protection exists — and for a reason that has nothing to do with this seam: M-05 anchored those nodes to align two baselines. Change that anchoring for any reason and the separation vanishes with no gate firing and no ruling breached** — **this seat's own words back at it, *two elements agreeing by convention rather than by construction*, T95's shape, which the lane itself cited when it built the draw's midpoint as a derivation.** **RULED: the seam between a scrolling region and a fixed block below it is a separation of INK, not of rects. Where the fixed block's topmost ink already stands clear of the clip line, no ground is reserved — and the clearance is GATED, never assumed.** **OWED: measure clip-line to topmost rendered ink, gate at ≥6px, report the figure.** **Costs no viewport — 177.9 stands and the build's numbers are undisturbed.** **Better than what was ruled: a reserve would have spent 6px to protect a property the layout already had; the gate protects it for nothing. The build found this by being built, which is what a build is for** | batch 81 |

| S84 | `DRAW {price}` under C46 — the 80% rule does NOT extend, because its remedy does not exist here | **RULED; ONE QUERY OWED** · DD 2026-08-15 batch 79, on the lane's measurement off the rendered control. **Widest observed over three boards `DRAW  +253` = 97.1px, 87% of the 112px cell; `AWAY  −341` (what the cell was sized for) = 91.4px, 82%; format ceiling `DRAW  +10000` = 115.5px, 103% — overflows.** **S77-am's 80% rule is TEMPTING HERE AND WRONG: its remedy clause is the whole rule — *a form over 80% is RE-AUTHORED, never accommodated* — and a stamp form is authored copy where a price is not.** `DRAW  +253` is a fixed label plus a generated numeral, and **nobody can re-author a number the model emits; extending a rule whose only remedy is unavailable would manufacture a PERMANENT VIOLATION instead of a fix**, leaving the cell in breach at 87% with nothing anyone is allowed to do. **RULED, the general discipline: a cell holding an AUTHORED string is sized against the authored population and re-authored when it misses; a cell holding a GENERATED string is sized against the REACHABLE MAXIMUM OF ITS GENERATOR — and what is reachable is a question for the MODEL, never a sample taken off the surface.** **Three boards is C46's own failure mode in C46's own words — *sweep the POPULATION, not the suspects*.** **OWED: one query — the maximum reachable draw price from `OddsMath.OverroundOdds` over the reachable latent range — not a digit count and not more boards.** **Why it is not obvious either way: a five-digit American price needs decimal ≥ 101, i.e. a draw under 1% probability, and the draw is structurally the LEAST extreme of the three outcomes since even a total mismatch keeps 0–0 and 1–1 live — so probably unreachable, but *probably* is not a measurement and the surface must not certify a cell on a plausibility argument about a model it does not own.** **And four digits is not free: at ~9.2px/digit `DRAW  +9999` measures ~106px = 95%, the tightest string on the board — recorded as a fact, not a defect.** **PRE-COMMITTED: (1) max ≤ 4 digits → the cell holds, S84 CLOSES with the tightness recorded and no change; (2) max is 5 digits → the cell must grow, which is a BOARD-GEOMETRY change on a board Allen has just fixed, so it returns to him rather than being taken here.** Not ruled: what a grown cell would cost — it does not exist until (2) fires | batch 79 |

| T99 | The stats panel over the scorebug — the FREEZE is the licence, the GOALS row is not | **RULED with a standing condition; one capture ordered** · DD 2026-08-15 batch 79, on TV's panel build. **TV's argument is sound and one of its two limbs is load-bearing while the other must not be relied on.** **THE OVERLAY IS PERMITTED AND THE FREEZE IS WHAT PERMITS IT: time is frozen while the panel is open, so the scorebug's facts cannot change behind it** — **a covered fact that cannot move is DEFERRED; a covered fact that can move is LOST**, and the ruling rests on that distinction entirely. **RULED: the panel may cover the scorebug band FOR AS LONG AS TIME IS FROZEN WHILE IT IS OPEN; if the match ever runs behind this panel, the scorebug must survive the overlay.** **Written as a standing condition rather than a one-time approval, because the danger is a future change that looks unrelated** — *let the match play while he reads the stats* is a plausible later improvement that would silently break this. **THE GOALS ROW IS NOT THE JUSTIFICATION: a statistic is not a result.** The scorebug's `0 — 0` is the match's STANDING; a stats table's GOALS row is one measure among possession, shots and corners — same digits, different roles. **The row STAYS** (goals-for is a legitimate table row) **but must not be offered as the reason the scorebug can be covered, because that claim makes the panel a REPLACEMENT, and a replacement owes everything the original carries — the score in its own form, the clock, and T38's single-frame change with no intermediate state. The panel does none of that and should not be asked to.** **Nothing is lost because the match is not moving, not because the score is printed twice.** **CAPTURE ORDERED, one, and it must NOT be at 0–0** — a stats panel over a goalless scorebug proves nothing, since the covered scorebug carries no information and no reading of it can fail; **shoot at a non-level scoreline after at least one goal.** **PRE-COMMITTED: (1) the panel reads as a DELIBERATE overlay, not one that overshot its zone; (2) the covered band shows no FRAGMENT of the scorebug — a half-covered scorebug is worse than a fully covered one, since a sliced score is a fact rendered unreadable where a hidden one is merely deferred; (3) the GOALS row does not read as the scoreline; (4) on close the scorebug returns with values unchanged — the freeze visible rather than asserted.** **TO CONFIRM RATHER THAN ASSUME: whether the EVENT STRIP is also covered** — T66 puts authored statements there and T87-am2 gave the drawn ending a minimum hold, and **a statement that fires and holds behind an opaque panel was never made**; if the strip is inside the covered zone it takes its own clause, because **the freeze protects the scorebug because its facts are STATIC, and a timed statement is not static even when the clock is** | batch 79 |

The gate's state decides whether the GATE needs repair, never whether the VALUE is in the palette. (2) **Decisive — the value is outside the room's warm family in BOTH spaces:** HSV 88° is yellow-green *by construction of the hexcone* (60° yellow, 120° green, amber 30–45°), and the one room emission whose triple room supplied, `(0.038, 0.032, 0.024)`, returns **34.3° HSV** — so the family sits near 34° HSV and 83–85° CIELAB while the authored value sits at 88° and 125.7°. **The space confusion explains how it PASSED; it did not create the failure** — `88.0` matched the numerals of a band drawn in another space, a coincidence of digits, not of colour. **Ruled — HUE ONLY, one variable:** the re-tint moves to **CIELAB hue 83–85°** per R41-am's standing form (*the swatch supplies hue and chroma; luminance is the element's own*). **Chroma NOT re-opened** — authored 26.62 held and rotated at constant C\*; the rendered delta (chroma +2.8, L\* +3.9) was never the defect, and bounding authored chroma against the room's RENDERED emitter band (≈5) is the two-instruments error this lane has paid for twice. **The firing half STANDS, GRANTED** — fires on the two settlement beats and nowhere else, both agreeing to two decimals on a pinned in-room set; clause 4's *"fires on settlement, not per leg"* holds, and room's note that both beats are LOSSES (`grammar-BreakawayAgainst`, `grammar-LegFinalLost`) confirms it is keyed to the moment, not to a win. **Batch 13's "~85–92°" is RESTATED to the owning doc's 83–85° (§1.5, this seat's)** — written four batches before C33-am3 with NO SPACE NAMED, and its upper half brackets no measured member (C18 §4.1); the owning document postdates it and already carries the family, so the stray number conforms to the document rather than a new decision being made. **Why green is wrong on design, not only arithmetic:** `TvLight` is C2's green — the SCREEN's hue — so a settlement glow at 125.7° makes the room answer the payoff moment by taking on the television's own colour, losing its own response at the one instant it most needs one. **Still owed, unchanged:** the cast on the derivation's own `housing above panel` box shot under **R23's recipe with `TvLight` disabled** is TV's half of the joint task and is NOT satisfied here — room correctly declined the rendered absolutes (confounded by the live panel green, §2.6; the DELTAS are sound evidence of firing, the ABSOLUTES close nothing). **Coupled:** the `[0.78, 1.06]` amplitude window sits in the same source comment as the 88.0° and re-derives with the conversion — ruled with V6, not assumed here **T65-am2 · batch 37 — batch 36's TARGET was wrong; the VERDICT is unchanged (§1.5, this seat's).** Batch 36 restated the band from "~85–92°" to **83–85°**, reasoning that its upper half bracketed no measured member. **Wrong, and V6's provenance is what shows it:** the band has two traced anchors — **laptop lid 85.1–85.3° at the bottom and the warm key tube ~92° at the top**, both rendered CIELAB, both from batch-13 records. **The key IS the upper anchor**; the band was correctly drawn. **The restatement to 83–85° is WITHDRAWN and the ruled band 85–92° stands as written.** Cause: batch 36 read the family off room's T65 record, which lists the SCREENS (lid, phone, laptop) and not the key — the key entered the record one docket later, in V6's trace. **Second error, the same class this seat warned room about in the same batch:** the band is a **RENDERED** band and `roomSettlementWarm` is an **AUTHORED** constant, so telling the lane to author to 83–85° compared an authored value against a rendered bound. **Corrected — the authored constant is the KNOB; the ruled band 85–92° is checked on the RENDERED CAST**, on TV's own box under R23 with `TvLight` disabled (the capture already owed, now carrying the check); **nobody authors to the band's number and assumes it renders there** — the authored↔rendered gap is real and non-uniform (1.9° on the lid, 10.7° on the key). **Unchanged and in force:** the violation itself (125.7° sits 33.7° past the band's top and is outside the room's warm family in BOTH spaces), the retirement of the provisional (88.0°, 0.9), hue-only with chroma not re-opened, the firing half granted, and T80-am's freeze. **What the pair of errors argues:** batch 36's finding that the VERDICT is independent of V6 was sound and stands, and room's hedge was aimed at the verdict where it was over-cautious — but the coupling that mattered ran to the **REMEDY**, and neither seat named it. *A coupled docket can be independent for the verdict and dependent for the fix* **T65-am3 · batch 51 — THE SUBJECT STAYS UNADJUDICATED; THE VIOLATION SURVIVES; THE TARGET IS VOID.** Room's R23 pair fired **disposition 3** and **the subject's 87.3–92.7 is NOT a pass and must not become one by the band moving underneath it** — returned unadjudicated (§2.6), re-judged **only** against a re-derived band (V6-am) on a run where the anchors separate. **Two things must not be quietly harvested from this: (1) the subject was never measured against a working instrument** — a band whose top is void cannot pass anything, and **a subject that happens to sit inside a WRONG band has not conformed to a RIGHT one**; **(2) resolution is still unsolved** — subject spread at **77% of the band** is C32's problem wherever the top lands, so the re-run addresses **signal**, not only the bound. **WHAT SURVIVES, stated because the arc otherwise looks unravelled: T65-am's VIOLATION FINDING STANDS.** `roomSettlementWarm` measures **125.7°**, **~24° above even the corrected anchor** and outside the family under **every** candidate top — 83–85, 85–92, or a band reaching 101.4. **The violation, the retirement of the provisional (88.0°, 0.9), hue-only with chroma not re-opened, and the granted firing half are all UNTOUCHED.** **What is void is the TARGET:** the remedy's direction — move toward the room's warm family — survives, **the number to move to does not exist yet and is not invented here**, and **per C41 the expectation stays a DIRECTION OF TRAVEL and never a value**, which is the clause keeping this ruling from making the same mistake a third time **T65-am4 · batch 52 — THE SUBJECT'S HUE HAS NEVER BEEN MEASURED, and batch 51's survival claim was WRONG (§1.5, third instance in this chain, all this seat's).** **The asymmetry that gives it away:** the key tube measures **authored 102.7° / rendered 101.4°, a gap of 1.3°**, while `roomSettlementWarm` measures **authored 125.7° / rendered 87.3–92.7°, a gap of ~35°** — **same instrument, same run, same space.** A 1.3° gap on one element and ~35° on another **is not a property of the elements; it is the two numbers measuring different things.** **THE DIAGNOSIS: the housing's absolute hue is a MIXTURE.** With `TvLight` disabled the panel green is gone, **but the key tube still lights that housing** — so the rendered figure is *the housing under key PLUS glow*, not *the glow*, and **an absolute on a lit surface can never isolate one contributor.** **THIS SEAT'S OWN R23 SPEC IS DEFECTIVE ON THE POINT:** Route A (absolute on the housing, `TvLight` disabled) was written as the RULED route and Route B (the on/off delta) as a fallback for a *different* reason — the coupling precondition. **Route B was the correct PRIMARY all along**, only the delta isolating the glow's contribution, and **room's instinct was right twice over**: T65-am already recorded that *the DELTAS are sound evidence and the ABSOLUTES close nothing*, **and this seat then built a spec whose primary route produces an absolute.** **THE CORRECTION TO BATCH 51:** it stated the violation survives because *125.7° is outside every candidate band* — which **compared an AUTHORED value against a RENDERED reference, the exact error T65-am2 identified and corrected, made again in the batch that was correcting the same class of error.** **RULED — the honest state, which is neither the violation standing nor the violation dead: the authored constant is 125.7°** (a fact about the code, not in dispute); **the glow's RENDERED hue is UNMEASURED** — not by the authored value (the knob, per T65-am2) and not by the housing absolute (a mixture); **so the violation is UNPROVEN, not disproven** — not withdrawn, and **not to be harvested as a pass**; **and a NEW finding stands on its own — the authored↔rendered relationship for this element is UNEXPLAINED**, 35° where a comparable element shows 1.3°, and **until that is explained neither number can serve as the check**, the explanation being worth having whichever way the verdict falls. **OWED: the DELTA** — glow-on minus glow-off, one frame, pinned and asserted, converted through the shared `linear_to_lab`, so the glow's own chromaticity is the difference's and the mixture problem disappears. **IT MAY ALREADY EXIST: if two halves of the four-half capture on main are glow-on and glow-off under one recipe, the delta computes from frames already shot and NO WINDOW IS NEEDED** — check that first, per the standing practice of checking existing crops before booking a capture; **if it does not exist, that is the window to book, and it is the only thing on this item needing one.** **Unchanged and not re-opened: the firing half (granted), the retirement of the provisional (88.0°, 0.9), chroma not re-opened — and the risk line, THE TOP BEING WRONG DOES NOT MAKE THE SUBJECT CONFORM. It still does not. It now also does not make it fail. BOTH readings are void and that is the actual position** **T65-cl · batch 53 — THE SUBJECT RULED; T65's REMAINING HALF CLOSES ON THE PROPERTY, and the numeric floor is RETIRED as a check on this element.** Room delivered both items without a window (`65858f8`). **ROOM'S REFINEMENT ADOPTED IN FULL, and it is better than this seat's reasoning (§1.5, fourth in this chain and the FIRST the lane corrected rather than the seat):** batch 52 carved the population **lights vs screens**, and **`PhoneBuzzLight` falsifies that in one member** — it is a **light**, it sits at **83.3°** with the screens, **and it does so because it IS the screen emitter rescaled.** **The correct carve is FIXTURE FAMILY — what shares an authored source belongs together** — and room's own sentence is the sharpest thing in the docket: ***clustering cannot defend the split, its own reasoning does.*** This seat argued from clustering (2° apart, therefore a family); **clustering is a CONSEQUENCE, not a cause**, and it cannot defend the split precisely because a LIGHT clusters with the screens. R39's shared base was always the cause and R39 said so (*closes by construction, `Amp(1/3/15)` off one shared base*). **The conclusion survives; the argument for it was wrong.** **Families on the right axis:** the screen emitter and everything rescaled from it (lid, laptop, phone, **`PhoneBuzzLight`**) at **83.3–85.4°**; the warm key tube at **101.4°**; `ArtIndicator` at **49.7°** (rust law); `WindowGlow` at **77.0°** (textured, the map governs, not a palette member). **WHICH FAMILY THE SUBJECT IS IN: NONE.** `roomSettlementWarm` is **its own authored constant**, neither rescaled from the screen base nor the key — **its own fixture, belonging to no existing family** — **which dissolves the question the band was asked to answer**, since a band bounds a family and **four batches were spent measuring this element against a family it was never a member of.** **THE ARITHMETIC, TAKEN AT FACE VALUE:** the subject sits **8.7° below at nearest, 14.1° at farthest**, the tolerance interval is **96.0–106.8**, and the nearest reading is **3.3° below the floor**. **That failure is NOT RESOLVABLE and this seat will not pretend it is** — a **3.3°** miss against a subject spread of **5.4°** is **smaller than the instrument's own noise**, which is disposition 3's resolution problem re-expressed exactly as room says: **a gate cannot fail a value by less than it can measure.** **But resolution is not the ruling, because the proxy is the wrong instrument regardless. C40 GOVERNS AND THIS IS ITS CONVERSE:** a passing proxy is never read as the property held, so **a FAILING proxy must equally not be read as the property VIOLATED.** **The property clause 4 protects is that the room's response to settlement is the room's OWN warmth, not a foreign colour** — and the glow renders at **87.3–92.7°, BETWEEN the room's two warm fixtures** (screen family 83.3–85.4, key 101.4), inside the room's own warm span, and **33–38° away from the TV green that motivated the original finding**, whose design argument was *a settlement glow at 125.7° makes the room answer the payoff moment by taking on the television's own colour*. **It does not. It answers in the room's own warmth. RULED: the property holds; T65's remaining half CLOSES.** **What the close does NOT do: it certifies no number, it does not resurrect the band as a bound on this element, and it does not convert a 3.3° miss into a pass** — it rules that **the element was measured against the wrong KIND of instrument, and that the thing the instrument stood in for is satisfied on the frames.** **Retired with it: the numeric floor as a check on `roomSettlementWarm`.** **ONE THING STAYS OPEN AND IT IS NOT BLOCKING — the authored↔rendered gap, 125.7° authored to 87.3–92.7° rendered, ~35°**, most likely **the grade**, which is exactly what T65-am2 warned of when it ruled *nobody authors to the band's number and assumes it renders there*; **the close does not depend on explaining it** because the law was always checked on the rendered cast, **but it is a standing hazard: on this element an authored value lands ~35° from where it renders, so nobody may author to a rendered target here without characterising the transfer first.** Nothing pending needs it |

under **C40's** proxy-versus-property distinction (*"money"* is the proxy, *changes in place* the property) a static money figure has no twitch to prevent, and it gains no column alignment either since RISK and PAYS sit side by side rather than stacked. **REFUSED anyway, and NOT on that reasoning — the exemption is unavailable BY CONSTRUCTION:** T75-am fixed the unit at the **font asset** and T82 confirmed the fix is cmap-only per asset, so since **`RiskPays` and `CashOut` are both condensed Bold 700 — one asset — and the cash-out figure ticks**, exempting RiskPays needs a SECOND condensed Bold asset for one slot, the shape refused three times (S70's fourth ground for one string, T77's second family for one slot, R41's second model). **Ruled: RiskPays takes tabular because its ASSET-MATE needs it** — the reasoning is recorded because it is sound and will recur, and the next person to raise it deserves to be told it is right about the mechanism and defeated by the asset. **Lever: span then size, never the copy** (§4 / T24-am); the value is T74's on its own frames, under this seat's constraint that **the ticket column's outer width does not move** (T46, R30) — relief comes from inside the row. **`LegRowLine0` passes by 3.4 px today and FAILS by 1.8 px on wiring** — the smallest number in the docket and the one that gates the wiring commit (T82-d). **The dead-backstop box and the wiring casualty are the SAME box:** with the backstop dead an overrun here would have shipped silently, and TV fixing it and re-measuring through the live measurer converts a silent defect into a loud one, which is the right direction. **But a working backstop is a DETECTOR, not a REMEDY — C40 again:** *truncation fires* is the proxy, *the string fits* is the property, and **G1's entire charter is that truncation is never reached** — so a LegRowLine0 truncating on the wiring commit is a violation that announces itself, not one that has been handled, and nobody may read "the backstop works now" as closure. **Ruled: 1.8 px of relief lands BEFORE wiring.** **`MARKET SUSPENDED`** routing unchanged from T84-am — span first by prior ruling (TV-12/13 grants it the slot exclusively); its number joins T74's table when it arrives. **THE INVENTORY IS SIX SLOTS SHORT AND SAYS SO** (C18 §4.1/§4.2, stated in the ruling rather than discovered later): **no slot list here is final, including the list of wiring casualties**, and the six land by name as additions to the same table, never a second document **T84-am3 · batch 43 — the `PEND` chip recorded, and a prior question that decides its remedy.** **Member recorded:** chip `PEND`, **6 px over**, **digit-free** — so tabular does not move it and it **holds the ship, not the wiring**, exactly per T82-d's split; routing unchanged (span, then size, never the copy), magnitude to T74's table. **But: is `PEND` AUTHORED, or is it `PENDING` ALREADY TRUNCATED?** Not cosmetic. **If authored** — a state word deliberately abbreviated for a chip — it is a 6 px overrun and nothing more, and T74 takes it. **If `PENDING` clipped** — the surface is displaying **an abbreviation nobody authored**, which is G1's class and T84's (the deck exists so truncation is never reached, and a state word is the last string that should be guessed at by a measurer); a state chip gets an **authored** short form, never a clipped one, **and the box would be over by considerably more than 6 px, since 6 px is the overrun of the ALREADY-SHORTENED string.** **DISPOSITIONS PRE-COMMITTED: authored → T74 with the others, no further ruling; truncated → it returns here as a COPY item, this seat authors the short form (G1's precedent — the DD owns the statement forms), and the 6 px is re-measured against the authored string rather than the clipped one.** **S65 is the neighbouring case worth reading first** — the laptop ruled `PENDING`'s treatment at composition level and settled that PENDING is structure; the TV's chip is the same word doing the same job one surface over. **Probe exit crash (x2) — acknowledged, post-phase, holds nothing, with ONE condition on the phrase "data intact":** R43 made it standing that a determinism or baseline result is **written to a file in the same step that produces it**; if the probe's output was written that way, *intact* is verifiable and this is bookkeeping — if it was recovered from a terminal buffer after the crash, it is an assertion about data from a process that then died, and **the sweep numbers resting on it inherit the doubt. Say which** — the whole T82 finding survived its own editor crash on exactly this distinction **T84-am4 · batch 44 — `PEND` is STRUCK, and the self-catch names a class that striking one row does not dispose of.** **STRUCK:** enumerated from every call site, the chip never renders `PEND` — nor `LIVE`, `WON` or `LOST`. The 6 px row was measured against **a string that cannot render**: no overrun, no remedy, no T74 entry. **TV caught this in its own instrument and reported it against its own number**, which is the discipline this register exists to reward. **The class:** the sweep tests each box against *the longest renderable form*, and its generator produced a form that is not renderable — **two directions follow and the second is the dangerous one.** **Over-generation** (strings tested that no call site can produce) costs phantom overruns and work scheduled against nothing; `PEND` is this, caught. **Under-generation** (strings a call site CAN produce that the generator never tested) costs **a box certified as fitting that overruns in play** — and **nothing about the `PEND` catch bounds that direction**, a generator not derived from call sites being able to fail both ways at once. **Ruled — a TRACEABILITY PASS, bounded to what matters: every sweep row whose number anyone has acted on or is scheduled to act on is confirmed against a call site, in both directions** — `RiskPays` (39.8 px, a live T74 magnitude), `LegRowLine0` (1.8 px, relief already landed and wiring-gating), `MARKET SUSPENDED`, and any row the six-slot additions produced. **Not a re-run of the sweep — an inventory naming its members** (C18 §4.1), the same deliverable V6 owes and for the same reason. **Named consequence, small but it should be said: `LegRowLine0`'s relief landed on a number this pass has not yet confirmed** — if it was measured against an untraceable string the relief may have been unnecessary or mis-sized; it is committed and harmless in the unnecessary direction, and it is why the pass covers ACTED-ON rows first rather than sweeping everything. **PROBE EXIT CRASH — CLOSED:** data written before the crash, corroborated twice with Unity out of the path, **the condition met exactly as stated** and no doubt inherited by the sweep numbers — R43's durable-file-in-the-same-step practice paying out, and the second time in two days a finding survived its own crash because the data was written where a crash could not reach it **T74-am3 · batch 56 — THE FIVE SURVIVORS DISPOSITIONED, and the money control's REAL defect.** **The pattern TV named is the most valuable line in the docket and it is ruled:** *the fourth consecutive correct ruling on this surface to make a box worse* is **not four numbers, it is one mechanism** — every correct ruling that adds a required element to a control makes a box sized before that requirement worse; **the rulings are not wrong and the boxes are not unlucky, the boxes encode an older contract.** Promoted at **C46-am**. **THE MONEY CONTROL: THE MEMBERS STOP SHARING ONE RECTANGLE.** Figure and status word are anchored from **opposite edges of ONE 241.0px box**, so **each one's slack is the other's overrun and any pair of long strings collides** — at rest 45.0, mid-tween 47.8, held preview 198.5 — **and `CASHED OUT $1,240` alone is 255.6, overrunning the box by 14.6 with NO second member at all.** **Ruled: figure and status word take SEPARATE ROWS**; §6.1's *one fixed rectangle owning all six states, never reflows* is untouched, **a two-row rectangle being still fixed and still never reflowing.** **Third time the same structural answer has been right** — the intervention prompt became a list, `TakeoverSub` becomes one, now this: **N INDEPENDENT STRINGS ARE NOT ONE ROW.** Height **measured before it lands**. **`MARKET SUSPENDED`'s 26.7px is likely retired by the same change** — it owns the slot exclusively (T43), so on its own row it has the full width; measured, not assumed. **THE FIVE (T89-D — resolved or signed, no silent survivors): `TakeoverSub` 174.8 — COMPOSITION, not span: it is a LIST**, rendering the ticket's legs joined, and TV correctly reports **it has NO BOUNDED WORST CASE AT ALL** — an unbounded string in a fixed box is not a sizing question; one leg per row, and **if capped the cap prints its own count** (C19's ruled exception). **`RiskPays` 47.5 — T74, with a named prior question: the payout magnitude is UNBOUNDED** (parlay-multiplied), and span or size cannot answer an unbounded string either — **a money-abbreviation ruling is owed and it is this seat's**, routed and not decided here. **`LegRowNeed0` 40.9 — T74, span then size**; bounded, digit-free, T84(a)'s own defect, **never the copy**. **`CashOut` 26.7 — expected to retire on the two-row composition**, re-measured there. **`LegRowState0` 4.7 — T74, span or size; TRACKING IS NOT THE LEVER**: .100em on this slot is ratified label treatment under the closed clause, and **un-ruling a ratified value to buy 4.7px is the move T85 exists to prevent.** **TWO NAMED UNBOUNDED CASES — `TakeoverSub` and `RiskPays` — are the sweep's honest limit**, disclosed by TV rather than found later; overrunning slots **7 → 5**, and one of the two that left did so **because a measurement was wrong rather than because anything was fixed** **T74-am4 · batch 57 — `RiskPays`: THE WORD *UNBOUNDED* IS THE WRONG ONE, and the composition is the fourth instance of one answer.** `PotentialPayout` is parlay-multiplied so the sweep correctly reports no bounded worst case — **but parlay multiplication is not UNBOUNDED, it is UN-ENUMERATED.** `MaxLegs` is finite (4), the stake has a ceiling, and every leg's odds come from a pricing model with its own range, so **the largest renderable payout is ARITHMETIC — a product of finite terms.** **Nobody has computed it, which is a different problem from it not existing**, and it is C18 §4.1's one level up: an inventory that does not name its members. **This seat is not designing around infinity when the number is a multiplication away.** **OWED, and cheap: the maximum renderable payout, computed from `MaxLegs`, the stake ceiling and the odds range** — then the box is sized against **the longest renderable form** (C46, T89-B) and this stops being a design question at all. **COMPOSITION RULED: `RISK` and `PAYS` take SEPARATE ROWS.** `RISK $1,234  PAYS $12,340` is **two labels and two figures in one row** at 296.5 in a 249.0px box — **N independent strings are not one row**, the fourth instance today after the intervention prompt, the money control and `TakeoverSub`; each figure then has the row's full width instead of sharing it with a sibling whose slack is its overrun. **ANCHORING, taken from this surface's OWN existing pattern: label left, figure RIGHT-ANCHORED.** Not invented — it is the clock's shape measured on the Phase T pair (T75-am2: *right-anchored, ink right edge constant while the left edge travels*): **a right-anchored figure grows LEFTWARD into its own row's slack rather than overflowing rightward off the box**, so the binding constraint becomes *figure meets label* rather than *figure meets box edge*, which is far later and is a constraint the composition can state. **It also pairs correctly with tabular figures** — T82 made digit advances equal, so a right-anchored tabular figure grows in **exact digit-width steps** and the label's clearance is predictable rather than content-dependent. **`RISK` is the stable half** (bounded by the stake ceiling) **and `PAYS` the variable one**, so only one row needs the headroom. **DISPOSITIONS PRE-COMMITTED on the number nobody has computed yet: the maximum renderable payout fits its right-anchored row → the box is sized against it, recorded as the longest renderable form, and this CLOSES with no further ruling; it does not fit → the span is re-derived ONCE AT DESIGN TIME** (§5.1 makes that explicitly legal) **or the composition changes again — and ABBREVIATION STAYS REFUSED IN BOTH BRANCHES**, a span that cannot be found making this a **C16 classification** where a wider row is a design cost and not a platform impossibility: **expensive, never impossible. Neither branch reopens C49** **T74-am5 · batch 59 — THE TWO-ROW FORM IS WITHDRAWN; ONE ROW, BOTH ENDS ANCHORED. Branch 2 triggers and the answer is inside batch 57's own other half.** **First, §1.5: this is C46-am violated by its author one batch after writing it** — that clause says *when a ruling adds an element to a control, the box's fit is re-derived IN THE SAME BREATH AS THE RULING* — and this seat ruled `RiskPays` into two rows **on WIDTH and re-derived no HEIGHT**, so two 24px rows need 60.0 in a 40.0 footer, over by 20. **Batch 57 ruled TWO things — separate rows AND label-left/figure-right-anchored — and the second makes the first unnecessary, which this seat did not see.** `RISK $1,234     PAYS $12,340` measures 296.5 **as one concatenated string with authored spacing in the middle**; **anchor `RISK` to the row's left edge and `PAYS` to its right and the authored gap CEASES TO EXIST**, the slack living between them where it costs nothing — **the binding constraint stops being 296.5 and becomes `RISK`'s ink plus `PAYS`'s ink**, the same content without the spacer. **RULED: ONE ROW, `RISK` left-anchored, `PAYS` right-anchored, the gap unauthored and variable.** **Better on its own terms, not merely cheaper** — two money figures at the two ends of a footer, breathing apart, is a cleaner composition than two rows crammed into 40px, and it keeps `PAYS` right-anchored so it grows leftward **in exact digit-width steps against the tabular set** (T82). **The height problem DISSOLVES rather than being paid for:** no band grows, no size moves, no deviation signs. **PRE-COMMITTED so the measurement decides without another round trip: the two inks fit 249.0 anchored → done, one row, no height question, nothing else moves; a box overrunning onto its neighbour is a COLLISION**, and collisions are what T84 blocks on, having been founded on two of them. **PRE-COMMITTED BOTH WAYS: (1) it lands in the control's own padding, touching nothing → SIGNED C16 DEVIATION** per T89-D's own pre-commitment that a signed deviation is acceptable for this grant — **named cost 3.0px of the money control's vertical margin, expiry the deferred sizing pass (T74), and THE PHASE PROCEEDS**, since holding a completed migration on 3px of padding is the shape C43's reasoning was written against; **(2) it lands on a NEIGHBOUR → it is a COLLISION and it is FIXED, not signed** — **leading is the first lever and it is cheap, line advance not being a ruled value on this surface** (§4.1 rules faces and sizes, not leading), so tightening 1.5px per row touches no ruled number and needs no T74, and only if leading cannot carry it does this become a size question. **On the four-element geometry option TV held: NOT ruled here, because it is not in evidence at this seat and this seat has refused to rule geometry it has not seen four times this week** — if branch 2 fires and leading cannot carry the 3px, **send it and it is ruled on sight**; TYPICAL: 124.7 + 145.9 = 270.6, OVER BY 21.6.** **Each half fits ALONE** (138.4 and 239.7 in 249.0); **it is the pair that does not.** **The one-row both-ends-anchored form was RIGHT and it is NOT ENOUGH** — it removed the authored spacer, which was the thing being measured rather than the content, and the remaining constraint is ink plus ink exactly as T74-am5 predicted. **That is a pre-commitment written as a TEST rather than a hope, returning the answer this seat did not want.** **And the fact floor is NOT a tail case: `$1,234` staked paying `$12,340` is a plain 10x parlay, so THE FOOTER COLLIDES AT ORDINARY VALUES** — the half that makes this urgent rather than theoretical. **RULED: `RiskPays` goes to T74 PROPER as its first named magnitude, with the fact floor 378.1px against a 249.0px row ON THE RECORD.** Stated plainly so T74 begins from the truth: **the fact floor exceeds the locked column by more than half the column again, so NO SPAN INSIDE THE CURRENT WIDTH CAN CARRY IT** — T74 is choosing between the ticket column's outer width (**locked** — T46, R30 — and a change there is Allen's) and a composition that does not put both money facts on one row. **All three pre-commitments hold and none is revisited: ABBREVIATION STAYS REFUSED (C49); the copy is not reopened (§4 / T24-am); the footer does not grow for a spacer that no longer exists.** **Added to this table by name on the frames rather than by assumption: `MARKET SUSPENDED` still visibly crosses the column's right edge** in both after-sets on every seed — T74-am3 expected the two-row change to retire its 26.7px and said *measured, not assumed*; measured, it is NOT retired, and its current number is owed |
