| T82 | The mandatory tabular figures are unreachable on the shipped stack | **RULED — the mandate STANDS unamended; the atlas ships the font's own tabular glyphs; the "cannot" is EXPENSIVE, not impossible** · DD 2026-08-12 batch 38, on TV's three-way measurement at `cb84278`. **Measurement ACCEPTED in full, and it corroborates harder than the docket claimed.** Three instruments, two sharing no machinery: an in-engine digit probe, `hmtx` advances read through `cmap` with no editor, and a GSUB enumeration — which is why the finding survives its own editor crash, named here as the discipline to copy. The docket left the file-versus-engine gap as a hand-wave (*"≈87 px against the 93 px measured, the difference being the instance"*); **checked at this seat it is exact.** Predicting each measured spread from `hmtx` and taking the ratio gives **1.0704 at Regular 36pt AND 1.0704 at Regular 28pt; 0.998 at Condensed 29pt AND 0.998 at Condensed 19pt** — **constant across sizes within each face, to four figures**, which is the signature of a uniform instance scale and therefore exactly the cause the docket named. The two instruments do not roughly agree, they agree exactly up to one scale factor per face, and **that scale factor IS the instance** (Condensed renders its file default; Regular renders an instance ~7% wider in advance). Proportionality is over-determined, not merely corroborated. **C44-clean** — reported as independent corroboration with the residual named, never as a bound judging a reading. **Classification (C16), because the headline and the body disagree:** the title says tabular *cannot be reached*; the body says *"unreachable by any in-scope choice"* and, decisively, *"closing it requires a decision about how the glyphs are obtained rather than a line of code"* — **that sentence IS C16's classification, stated by the lane, and it is adopted as this ruling's reasoning.** `tnum` at runtime is genuinely **impossible** (TMP's `OTL_FeatureTag` declares only `kern`, `liga`, `mark`, `mkmk`; no tag or property exposes one) — but tabular figures on this surface are **possible**, because the glyphs are drawn, present in GSUB and addressable at build time. Therefore **expensive, not impossible: no signed deviation is issued and none is needed.** The headline overshoots the body; recorded as a classification correction and nothing more — the lane surfaced the refuting route itself and recommended it, the opposite of a finding that hides its own escape. **The mandate is NOT amended:** the twitch is 46–93 px across ten digits at rendered sizes, not marginal; **T11 selected the face on this exact criterion** with Saira disqualified for lacking `tnum`, so amending now would retroactively decide the criterion the face was chosen on does not matter; and a faithful route exists — C16 does not let convenience reclassify itself as impossibility. **Option (b) `<mspace>` REFUSED** — forcing one advance imposes a metric **the family does not contain**, which is §4's *no synthesised styling* in the spacing channel (a synthesised bold is a smear, an italic a shear, a monospace the third of the same thing), and it hits letters in mixed strings: `CASH OUT $183` is the money control that cost four batches at T63/T68/T68-am/T71, and monospacing its label to fix its figures is **C10's shape exactly**. **Option (c) amend-the-mandate REFUSED, held as the named fallback with its condition PRE-COMMITTED before the build attempt:** if (a) proves unbuildable — the substitution cannot be resolved at generation time, or the tabular glyphs are not addressable in the generated set despite their GSUB presence — the classification changes to genuinely impossible and (c) returns as a **C16 signed deviation with a named cost and an expiry**; not before, and never on difficulty. **Option (a) GRANTED as the route** — the atlas ships the font's own tabular digits as its default set, the substitution resolved at build time with U+0030–0039 mapped to the tabular glyph indices. These are the figures **the type designer drew**, which is what separates it from (b). **The ruling is the OUTCOME, not the mechanism:** either the Unity generator resolving the substitution or a pre-processed static instance with the feature frozen and committed as the atlas source — **the lane picks**; this seat does not choose a build method. **Four conditions.** (1) **The unit is the ASSET, not the slot** — T75-am established it and this confirms it one level down; **every generated asset any figure slot renders on takes the tabular set, and the inventory NAMES ITS MEMBERS** (C18 §4.1), not "the atlas": the condensed Bold 700 asset carries `CashOut`, the surface's money figure, and an inventory naming two assets while missing a third ships a control that still twitches. (2) **Digits alone satisfy the mandate** — digits are the characters that change, `$`, `:` and separators are constant and need nothing; the mandate's content is **equal advance among digits**, not constant string width, so a score going 9 to 10 adds a character and widens and that is layout (T75-am2's right-anchor), never a tabular failure. (3) **One variable** — `lnum` is also present; figure STYLE does not move in this commit, tabular only. (4) **§3.5 binds the same commit** — a width change obliges the layout depending on it to be re-derived in the same commit, and a zone that cannot hold the widened figure routes to T74, never a tune inside the tabular commit. **Licensing: no new question** (same OFL 1.1 files already subsetted into SDF atlases; a build-time feature application is the same class of operation on the same files) — informational, since T11 recorded a licence basis. **Free by-product, ROUTED not ruled:** the constant per-face ratio is a **width-axis fingerprint of the rendered instance obtained without frames**, which is the desk measurement T78-am owes — bounded twice so nobody over-reads it: **advance alone cannot separate weight from width** (a heavier instance at one width is wider too, which is why T78-am asked for stem width AND width axis; this supplies one channel), and **which asset `cb84278` loaded decides what the number means** (at T-5 the canon faces may already be wired, in which case the ratio describes the migrated instance, not the legacy base T78 asked about). Lands in the deferred sizing pass with the rest of T78-am. **Sequencing: the build lands AFTER the after-set is shot** — not in Phase T, whose variable is a closed list of three under T81 with C43-am(3) forbidding anything joining afterwards; frozen in the interim per **T80-am2**. **FALSIFICATION PRE-COMMITTED, before the evidence was read (§1.5's discipline, and the reason it is stated here rather than after):** the after-set landed at `dd-import/tv-phase-t-after-2026-08-12/` while this ruling was being transcribed, and it **carries `clock-strings.tsv`** — the harness instrument owed since batch 34, now built. This ruling makes a checkable prediction about that file. At the moment of writing, **this seat has counted the set's files and opened none of them.** Pre-committed both ways: **within a group of equal-character-count clock strings in the right-anchored slot, the left ink edge MOVES** — that is what proportional digits mean and it is what T82 asserts; observing it **corroborates** T82 and T75-am3 and closes the `Clock` carve-out on rendered evidence as well as on the files. **If the left ink edge is instead INVARIANT within an equal-character-count group, T82's premise is wrong on the rendered surface** and this whole ruling re-opens — the atlas grant, the mandate's delivery clause and T75-am3's corrected disposition together — because the shipped stack would then be reaching a tabular advance by some path none of the three measurements found. Not a caveat: a stated way to be wrong, on evidence already on disk **T82-cl · batch 39 — THE FALSIFIER FIRED AND T82 SURVIVED IT.** Measured at this seat on the after-set's `clock-strings.tsv` plus its frames: 3-char `DD'` group **left-edge spread 22.0 px** where tabular predicts ~0; **correlation between ink width and the `hmtx` predicted advance +0.9824**; **residual sd about the fit 1.04 px**; narrowest `71'` 58.0 px to widest `90'` 80.0 px, **measured delta 22.0 px against 21.2 px predicted from the font's own advance table**; clock right edge spread 5 px, so the right-anchor holds. The left edge moves, and it moves by the amount the advance table predicts, to about a pixel. **A third instrument, sharing nothing with the two that produced the finding, in a third place.** Coverage stated (C28): 54 of 156 non-empty-clock frames measured, 37 in the clean subset, the remainder dropped where bloom merged the separator isolating the clock; the instrument's resolution is the 1.04 px residual, not the raw detection spread. **T75-am3's corrected disposition is confirmed on rendered evidence** — the left edge moves under the canon face, so the mandate is unmet by the STACK exactly as ruled, not by `Clock`'s face assignment **T82-b · batch 40 — the DERIVED FONT LANDED; source verified, and one line closes it.** `tnum` resolved at build time, **every digit exactly 1000 units, spread 0, cmap-only change** — the atlas route built exactly as granted: the font's own tabular glyphs, no outline invented, and a cmap-only diff is the narrowest possible expression of it. The unit is right, the change landing on the **asset** where T75-am put the property. **§2.5 holds the close open by one line** — *a source string is not a rendered element*; "every digit 1000 units, spread 0" measures the FILE, which is **necessary and not sufficient**, and is the same clause that made T82 a ruling rather than a note. **What closes it, with the instrument already built:** T75-am3's rendered line — within a set of equal-character-count clock strings in the right-anchored slot, **the left ink edge is INVARIANT**; the after-set already proved that instrument works by measuring the proportional case at 22.0 px spread on a 1.04 px residual, and the tabular case is the same measurement expecting ~0. **If TV's report carries that line, T82 CLOSES on this evidence; if not, one line is owed and nothing else.** **Still owed regardless, unchanged from the grant: the ASSET INVENTORY naming its members** — every generated asset any figure slot renders on, confirmed tabular, **including the condensed Bold 700 asset carrying `CashOut`**; a cmap-only change is per-file, three assets need three confirmations, and an inventory naming two of three is the defect C18 §4.1 exists for **T82-c · batch 41 — T82 does NOT close, and batch 40's close condition was this seat's to get wrong (§1.5).** TV's answer is correct and is the answer §2.5 was asking for: the derived font is proven tabular **at the font layer**, the shipped assets are **NOT wired to it** (held per the ruled sequencing). So **"mechanism accepted" matches the evidence and "the surface renders tabular" does not** — nothing on screen has changed, and **the rendered left-ink-edge line cannot run meaningfully until wiring** because there is nothing for it to measure. **Batch 40 wrote the close condition as though the assets were wired; they were not, precisely because this seat sequenced T82 after the pair and TV held that line.** The condition was not wrong about the INSTRUMENT, it was wrong about WHEN IT COULD RUN — the small version of the error C41 was written for, predicting a measurement against a state that does not exist yet. **State recorded precisely: mechanism GRANTED and verified at the font layer; surface UNCHANGED; the rendered line NOT OWED until wiring**, at which point it is one line on an instrument already built and already proven on the proportional case. **WIRING — GRANTED, and sequenced: after the sweep reports, not during.** One constant plus a regenerate; it waits on nothing else, the pair being shot and the freeze lifted. **It must not land mid-sweep** — G1-am4 ruled that a sweep running before the font lands has its digit-bearing rows voided, and landing the font INSIDE a running sweep does the same damage from the other direction, with this seat's instruction as the cause **T82-d · batch 42 — THE WIRING WORD: NOT YET, on exactly two conditions, both small and both already running.** **(1) `LegRowLine0`'s 1.8 px lands first** — wiring is otherwise a commit that CREATES a truncation on the leg statement, authorised by this seat, on a box the sweep named in advance, which is the one outcome the sweep existed to prevent. **(2) The six-slot enumeration completes** — an incomplete inventory cannot certify that LegRowLine0 is the ONLY wiring casualty, and a second one found after the commit is C18 §4.1 collecting on a debt this seat chose to run. **The principle behind the split, stated so it generalises: WIRING IS GATED ON THE FAILURES IT CREATES, NOT ON THE FAILURES THAT ALREADY EXIST.** `RiskPays` is 16.1 px over TODAY — that is T84's blocker, it already holds the ship, and it does not additionally hold the wiring; `LegRowLine0` passes today and fails on the commit, so it does. **On the word: send the enumeration result and LegRowLine0's relief together and the wiring word follows immediately** — it needs no further ruling, only those two facts; if the enumeration turns up further wiring casualties the same test applies to each, and none needs a fresh docket **T82-cl · batch 58 — CLOSED. The rendered half was already done, on the other case.** T82-c held the close open for the left-ink-edge line; TV delivered instead that **the sweep's tabular screen is a NO-OP on every digit-bearing row**, proportional and tabular measurements being identical on the wired tree. **Accepted, and stronger than what was asked — but only because the chain was already calibrated, which is the part worth writing down.** The left-ink-edge test measures the **consequence** (ink position) of equal advances; the screen measures **the advances themselves**, which is what the mandate is about. **§2.5 would normally hold this open** (a component measurement is not a frame) **and does not here, because T82-cl already measured the chain on the PROPORTIONAL case** — correlation **+0.9824** between measured ink width and the font's own advance table, residual **1.04px**, on this surface's own frames — so **the link from advance to rendered ink is MEASURED, not assumed**, and a component measurement of uniform advances lands on frames within about a pixel. **The wiring is confirmed by the same output:** the screen being a no-op means the derived font is **not merely present in the tree, it is what the surface renders.** **Residual risk named rather than waved:** digit-pair kerning could in principle move ink where advances are uniform, and T89-A's after-frames will corroborate incidentally — **corroboration, not a condition; the close does not wait on it.** **Endorsed as the best kind of instrument fix:** the sweep printed `no digits` both for a string with **no figures** and for one whose figures **screen to zero**, so `RISK $1,234  PAYS $12,340` was printing *no digits* — **the label was hiding the exact confirmation this condition asks for**, which is C18 §4.2's shape inside a log line | batch 38 |

locked odds make the read the player's job*); this is very likely a different quantity — the chance an action succeeds, not the match read — in which case T16 does not reach it. **Checked, not assumed:** the lane states which quantity it is, and if it is match-derived it falls under T16 with no new ruling **T86-am · batch 47 — T16's ban REACHES, but not by extension; the principle underneath it is RULED GENERAL.** TV reports `(99%)` is **the same quantity class as T16's subject at a different site** — a leg's probability at a decision prompt rather than on the momentum tape — and asks whether T16 reaches beyond the tape. **It does, but *does ruling X reach site Y* is exactly the question S67 was written about** (*a ruling can only reach the call sites that route through the thing it was ruled on*); T16 was ruled on the tape, extending it by analogy is the move S67 warns against, and **the honest way to cover the second site is to rule the principle directly and name T16 as its first instance.** **THE PRINCIPLE: the match theatre prints FACTS and OFFERS. It does not print OPINIONS.** A **price is an offer** — a thing the house stands behind, which the player can transact against; **a probability is the house's opinion** about an outcome with nothing attached to it. The player can take or leave a price; he can only agree or disagree with an opinion, and **this surface does not ask him to do that.** **This explains T16, T23 and T32(4) in one line** — win-probability numeral out, backed-player numeral out, the 10px numeral deleted — where before there were three separate deletions that happened to rhyme; **T16's own second reason IS this principle already** (*locked odds make the read the player's job*). **So `(99%)` GOES**, at the decision prompt as on the tape, for the same reason: it is the machine's read handed to the player, in a game whose skill is forming that read himself. **THE CONSTRUCTIVE HALF, because the counter-argument deserves an answer rather than a refusal:** at a frozen moment with three options, a player with no information is flipping a coin rather than deciding — true. **The answer is that the basis must be an OFFER, not an OPINION: if `SEND TO REVIEW` costs something, print THE COST.** That is a fact he transacts against, it belongs on this surface, and it makes the choice real — **`SEND TO REVIEW — $40` is a decision; `SEND TO REVIEW (99%)` is an instruction wearing a number.** Everything else he needs is already on screen and is his to read: score, clock, legs, prices, state. **ROUTED NOT RULED, two things visible from here: (1) a 99% option contains no decision** — whatever it costs, an option that succeeds ~always is pressed ~always, a balance question for the lane that owns the mechanic, named only because it was visible in the string; **(2) three key-bound COMMIT actions on the TV sit against T22's boundary** (*commit is an act on the laptop; the TV's gesture is a preview and release is never confirm*) — if `InterventionPrompt` genuinely commits from the theatre it **needs its own ruling rather than inheriting T22's**, because T22 drew that line deliberately; flagged, not ruled, the mechanic not being in evidence at this seat **T86-am2 · batch 56 — ATTRACT: one state COMPLIANT, two VIOLATIONS, one authored replacement.** TV **enumerated the STATES rather than the strings, which is the question that was asked**, and **checked the shop branch's hue rather than assuming it — expecting a violation and reporting its absence**; states 1 and 2 need no ruling. **STATE 3 `SIT TO WATCH THE SWEAT` — COMPLIANT, and TV's own doubt is answered:** it IS an instruction, and **§8 permits exactly this — *second person only in GENUINE instructions*.** The sweat genuinely waits for him to sit; | T87 | The final beat of a drawn match, and the word that must not reach the player | **RULED** · DD 2026-08-12 batch 49. **`decisive` is an ENGINE term and it never prints.** The engine's *P(home given decisive)* is exact and correct INSIDE the engine, where *decisive* means *not a draw*; on the surface it is **a rig string in a player slot — T31 and R38's class, the fourth instance.** A player has no concept of a decisive match; he has a scoreline. **Ruled: no player-facing slot ever prints `decisive`, or any partition term the engine reasons with** — if beat selection reads a `decisive` flag internally that is fine and invisible, and what is refused is **the word reaching a slot**. **THE BEAT ITSELF: THE WHISTLE, NOT A VERDICT — AND NOT NOTHING EITHER.** A drawn match has no goal to end on, and two failure modes sit either side of it, both already ruled against: **manufacturing a climax** is celebration, closed by T35 and T40 on mechanism rather than taste (a flourish spends the ration on a moment that did not earn it); **rendering nothing** makes a resolution draw as an absence, which reads as a bug and is **T84's class exactly** — the surface looks broken and the player learns a false rule. **Ruled: the beat is the match ENDING LEVEL, STATED** — the scoreline holds at its level value, the event strip states the fact at its own L2 tier (T66), the legs resolve to their words. **The theatre reports; it does not editorialise about a quiet ending.** **THE HALF THAT MATTERS MOST: a draw is quiet for the ROOM and LOUD for ONE TICKET.** A draw-backer **has won**; his leg lands like any other winning leg, and **the absence of a goal is not the absence of his result.** The single worst outcome available here is a surface that conflates *no goal* with *no result* and drains the one player whose ticket just came in. **The machinery already handles this correctly and must not be re-solved: T65's settlement glow FIRES ON SETTLEMENT, NOT ON A GOAL** — room's own record notes both observed beats are LOSSES, confirming it is *keyed to the moment, not to a win* — so a drawn match settles and is already a first-class settlement moment in the room. **Nothing new is needed; nothing existing may be narrowed to exclude it** **T87-am · batch 65 — THE SCORELESS DRAW: T87 REACHES IT. No new beat, one risk named, frames owed.** The queued question was whether 0–0 needs its own read: **it does not need its own RULING, and it does need its own LOOK** — different things, and separating them is the whole of this amendment. **T87 REACHES 0–0, and the reason is MECHANICAL rather than a judgement call: every mechanism T87 names is GOAL-INDEPENDENT**, so none is absent at 0–0 — the scoreline **holds a value it already holds** (0–0 is a level value like any other); the event strip **states a fact** available without a goal; the legs **resolve to their words** (S74's `LEVEL AT FULL TIME`/`LEVEL` for a draw-backer, `{CLUB} TO WIN`/`LEVEL 0–0` resolving LOST for a team-backer — **and `LEVEL 0–0` is already rendering correctly, read on the closing frames**); and **T65's settlement glow FIRES ON SETTLEMENT, NOT ON A GOAL**, room's own record confirming it is keyed to the moment rather than to a win, **so a goalless match still settles as a first-class settlement moment in the room.** **RULED: no new beat is authored for 0–0, and nothing existing may be narrowed to exclude it** — T87's own clause, repeated because **a goalless match is exactly the case a narrowing would quietly drop.** **WHAT 0–0 CHANGES IS THE RISK, NOT THE RULE, and it is why this earned a read.** T87 named two failure modes: manufacturing a climax (closed by T35/T40) and **rendering nothing, which *makes a resolution draw as an absence, reads as a bug and teaches a false rule*.** **At 0–0 the second risk is at its MAXIMUM and only at 0–0:** in a 1–1 the surface has punched, the scorebug has changed, the room has moved, so the ending arrives against a match that visibly happened — **in a 0–0 NOTHING has punched all match**, so a quiet ending arrives against a quiet match, and **the one state this surface must never be mistaken for is IDLE.** **The checkable question, stated so the read has a subject: is the stated ending DISTINGUISHABLE FROM THE SURFACE SIMPLY IDLING?** Not *is it loud* — T87 forbids that — **but is it legible as a resolution.** **AND THE HALF THAT MATTERS MOST, restated because 0–0 is where it is easiest to lose: a draw is quiet for the ROOM and LOUD for ONE TICKET.** The draw-backer **has won**, on a match where nothing happened, and **his leg lands like any other winning leg, at the same treatment, on the same settlement moment.** **The worst outcome available is a surface that reads a goalless match as a non-event and drains the one player whose ticket just came in** — 0–0 being the precise case where that error is cheapest to make. **NOT RULED — THE READ ITSELF, because no frame shows it: no 0–0 FULL-TIME frame exists in evidence at this seat**, the `LEVEL 0–0` readings being **mid-match** (11', 32') which is the progress line doing its job and says nothing about the ending. **C11 binds — a claim about how the ending reads is made against a rendered frame OF THE ENDING.** **OWED: a goalless match to full time — the settlement beat, the event strip's full-time line, the room's glow, and BOTH tickets' legs resolving** (a draw-backer's and a team-backer's, so the loud half and the quiet half sit in one set). **DISPOSITIONS PRE-COMMITTED so this costs one window: (1) the ending is legible as a resolution and the draw-backer's leg lands at full treatment → T87 covers 0–0 with no amendment and this CLOSES; (2) the ending reads as the surface IDLING → it is T87's own named failure mode and the remedy is the EVENT STRIP stating the fact**, which T87 already assigns it, **never a flourish** (T35/T40 closed that on mechanism); **(3) the draw-backer's settlement is MUTED relative to a goal-won leg → that is a defect and it is the SERIOUS one**, fixed at the settlement path and never by | T88 | The intervention prompt commits an irreversible spend on one frame of input, and its copy promises otherwise | **RULED — VIOLATION; the queued T22 question, which the find turns from a boundary case into a defect** · DD 2026-08-12 batch 50. **The copy says `HOLD`. The input is `wasPressedThisFrame`. A single press commits an irreversible spend the instant it fires.** **FOUR VIOLATIONS STACKED, and they are not one violation four times: (1) the copy names a SAFETY PROPERTY the input does not provide** — `HOLD` promises a preview and an abandon path and the input has neither; **worse than a wrong label**, because a wrong label misnames a thing while this misdescribes what happens when you touch it, **and the player relies on that description exactly when he is careless**. **(2) T22(c) violated — release is never confirm**, except here there is no release at all: the press IS the commit, so **there is no interval in which the player is holding something he can still abandon** — T22's safety structure is ABSENT, not weakened. **(3) T22(d) — commit is an act on the laptop**, and this commits money from the theatre. **(4) There is NO ABANDON PATH:** the first frame of input is terminal, **on a surface the player is WATCHING rather than operating**, and a control that can only be fired and never backed out of, on a seated-posture surface, **will be fired by accident.** **T59 ALREADY RULED THE PRINCIPLE ONE AXIS OVER** (*display state and input state are the same state, read from one value; accepting a declared-refused input on a money control is the worst outcome — the player gets a price the display is not showing*): T59 governed AVAILABILITY, this is the same law on the **GESTURE** axis, and **a player acting on the displayed contract gets an outcome the display did not describe** — T59's own sentence with *gesture* for *price*. Promoted to **C48**. **RULED: (a) the theatre MAY carry this commit** — T22's *commit is an act on the laptop* is not overridden, because **T22 already contemplated a theatre-side confirm and specified its shape** (*a second key during the hold; no timer, no auto-commit*), and the mechanic earns it: **a frozen shot that requires walking to the laptop is not a frozen shot.** **(b) EVERY SPENDING OPTION TAKES T22's FALLBACK GESTURE, UNCHANGED** — hold to preview, the preview showing what the option does **and what it costs** (T86-am: the basis for a decision is an offer, not an opinion); **release abandons, always**; a second key during the hold commits; **no timer, no auto-commit; a press does not commit on any of them.** **(c) The DECLINING option is not a spend and does not take the gesture** — `LET IT DIE` costs nothing and **is already what happens if the player does nothing**, so a single press is proportionate; ruled as the general shape, **the weight of the gesture matches the weight of the act**, which also stops the three reading as peers when two spend and one does not. **(d) Until the gesture is built the copy must NOT say `HOLD`** — a label describing a safety the input lacks is the defect, **but shipping the honest label on a press-to-commit control is worse than either, so the fix is the GESTURE and not the word**; if the gesture cannot land in this pass **the control does not ship**, a money control firing on one frame not being a state this surface may be seen in. **THE 380px — the answer is neither copy nor size: 635px carrying a string needing ~1015px is not a sizing miss but a composition that does not fit its zone**, which is **C46's named class** (a fixed box's unstated fits-assumption, failing). **Ruled from existing law rather than invention: THREE OPTIONS ARE A LIST, NOT A LINE** — S24 ruled exactly this shape for the scorer market (*renders as a single-column offer list, never a paired row with a dead cell*) and the reason transfers, **N offers being a list while one line is a row pretending to be a sentence**. It **solves the gesture problem in the same move**: one option per row gives each its own cost, its own key, and somewhere for a hold affordance to live. **NOT routed to T74, and the distinction matters** — T74 settles which SIZE AUTHORITY governs and nothing here asks whether the type is too big; **line-to-list is COMPOSITION, which is this seat's**, and §3.5 makes re-deriving a layout at design time explicitly legal. **Condition, stated because this seat does not have the zone: a list needs vertical room, and if the prompt's zone cannot carry three rows that returns here WITH THE ZONE'S DIMENSIONS** as a composition question, never a copy trim — §6's fixed grid still binds and the zone does not resize to content. **THE WORDING IS NOT RATIFIED: this seat does not ratify strings it has not read.** S68 is the standing lesson and it has been applied twice in two days (T85-am3 held two slots proposed on a class description rather than their text, and one of this seat's own stated reasons proved wrong while the disposition still fired) — **a voice ratification is made against the string, never against a description of it.** Send the strings and they rule on sight; **send the 380px breakdown with them** (options line alone, or the whole prompt including `SHOT FROZEN`), because the list ruling changes the arithmetic and the two are answered differently. **Nothing blocks** — the gesture ruling and the list direction are both actionable now and neither waits on the wording **T88-am · batch 56 — THE ZONE, THE KEY, THE STRINGS — and a defect in the fix that is C48's OWN defect.** **`HOLD N LET IT DIE` IS WRONG BY THE RULING THIS ARC PRODUCED:** T88(c) ruled the declining option **does not take the gesture** (it costs nothing and is already what happens if he does nothing, so a single press is proportionate), the string was carried forward under *unchanged* and **never met T88(c)** — **so the prompt prints `HOLD` over an input that is a PRESS, which is precisely the copy-promises-a-gesture-the-input-does-not-implement defect C48 was written for, surviving inside the pass that fixed it.** **RULED: `N LET IT DIE`**, no `HOLD`; the two spending rows keep theirs and **the difference between the rows now does real work — it tells him which options cost him something.** *Recorded without blame — it was listed as unchanged and unchanged was true; **a string that never meets the ruling that changed its class is the same defect one level up**, and the *listed so the set is the whole set* section is where it was caught.* **THE 20px: `SHOT FROZEN` LEAVES THE ZONE; THE ZONE DOES NOT GROW.** Four reasons stacking: **the stage already says it** (the shot is frozen on screen, this surface shows state and lets him read it — T33's shape — and a title announcing what the frame displays is the restatement class S37/S58/T69/T70 each ruled); **growing the zone spends a locked dimension to restate it** (R30, and §6's grid does not resize to content); **it removes the worst case rather than affording it** — three option rows measure 82.5 in a 90.0 zone and fit **in EVERY ownership combination**, where C46 forbids leaning on the common case; and the held preview keeps 35.0 spare. **If a word is still wanted the EVENT STRIP is its home** (§8's *one authored line explaining the latest move*, which is what a frozen shot is) — **measured before it lands, not asserted** (T89's bar), and not required, since the frame may carry the moment alone. **THE CONFIRM KEY: `ENTER` — RATIFIED**, both halves of TV's reasoning adopted: **bound to nothing** in the room lane's shared action asset so the gesture reserves no key the room uses, and **one commit key across both money controls** keeps the two contracts readable as the same contract — **standing: the studio has ONE commit key, not one per control**; a re-bind is one line plus two strings, which is the right shape for a decision that may later meet a platform this seat has no evidence about. **STRINGS RATIFIED:** `HOLD M MULLIGAN (ONE MULLIGAN SLIP)` (T88(b)'s cost, in `RelicCatalog`'s own name and the whistle's ruled parenthetical form, **no abbreviation coined** — G1's defect class avoided rather than survived); `ENTER CONFIRMS · RELEASE ABANDONS` (**states BOTH halves of the contract**, which is what C48 asks of a label; declarative, not second person; the register's own separator); `ENTER TO CASH OUT` (**ratified as copy** — and it breaks its box, see T74-am3). **`ENTER TO CASH OUT`'s reasoning is ENDORSED and is the lane applying C48 to a case this seat did not rule:** *under a held preview, `HOLD E` tells the player to do the thing he is already doing and never says how to finish it.* **A contract that describes the previous moment is not a contract.** **THE LANE'S OWN DERIVED RULE RATIFIED AND NAMED — *the gesture acts on what is shown*:** it is **T59's law on the gesture's TIME axis** (display state and input state are one state), since **a hold that outlives the thing it was started against is a press on a screen that has moved.** Standing precondition on every held gesture | batch 50 |

Dispatched to TV **Batch 22: implementation NEEDS FRAMES** — FitToColumn's greens are source-side; the deck's fit is confirmed on rendered frames in the scheduled TV window before the grant **GRANTED · CLOSED · batch 27** — the deck rendered fit on frames; T70-am BTTS flag withdrawn as the DD error **G1-am · batch 35 — the COPY holds; the FIT certification is void and re-certifies on the after-set (ruled at T81).** TV named "G1-copy re-authoring" as a known consequence of landing the canon face; **scope corrected DOWNWARD, and canon already says so** — §4/T24-am: *"Where a weight or face change makes a string overrun, the remedy is the size or the span — NEVER THE COPY; §8's authored forms exist so truncation is never reached."* **Stands unchanged** (face-independent copy decisions): both strings per leg, the club-word and surname conventions, the authored fallbacks, and AnytimeScorer's `NOT YET`/`SCORED` pair-defect fix. **Void:** the batch-27 FIT grant, granted on frames in the old face — C18 §4.1, *a gate certifies the geometry it ran against and any change to that geometry voids the certification*; R22's shape exactly. **Re-certifies on the after-set's own frames — a RE-MEASUREMENT, not a re-authoring.** **If a string overruns:** the remedy is the span or the size and **both routes sit OUTSIDE Phase T** — record the overrun on the after-set, route it to the deferred sizing pass (T74), do not re-author copy and do not re-tune inside the pair. Authoring reopens ONLY if a string overruns and the span route cannot carry it — a finding the after-set produces, never an assumption to build on now. *(Row moved from the Phone table to TV 2026-08-11 batch 35 — bookkeeping, ID unchanged per C22.1; scope is "TV ticket column only" and it was never a phone item.)* **G1-am2 · batch 38 — the fit certification faces a SECOND geometry change; the after-set does NOT close the whole deck.** G1-am voided the batch-27 fit grant under C18 §4.1 and set it to re-certify on the after-set — which is shot with **proportional** digits, after which T82 **widens** them. Several authored forms carry figures (Total Goals, Total Corners, Total Cards, and the price and money forms), so those strings meet a second geometry change AFTER the re-certification meant to close them. **Ruled: the after-set re-certification is FINAL for figure-free strings and PROVISIONAL for any string containing a digit**, the latter re-certifying once more when T82's assets land — same clause as before, a gate certifies the geometry it ran against. **Remedy unchanged and still outside Phase T:** an overrun is answered by the span or the size and routes to T74, **never the copy** (§4 / T24-am); the authored deck stands. Third geometry event on one certification, named so no one reads the after-set grant as final **G1-am3 · batch 39 — the re-certification RESULT, recorded; and a correction to the gate's own failure text.** **Result ACCEPTED in full:** the NEED column does not hold in the canon face — both at-budget forms (`ONE TEAM SCORELESS`, `ONE TEAM BLANKED`) miss 249 px, as do three of six authored statements; the compact column re-certifies at 143 px. **The lane's digit-split is ADOPTED and is sharper than the ruling it answers** — G1-am2 made every digit-bearing string provisional, and the lane observed that **every NEED-column miss is digit-free, so the failure is FINAL and will not move when T82 lands**, while the compact column's PASS is the provisional half. Correct, and better stated than the ruling that asked for it. **Copy is NOT reopened** (§4 / T24-am, *never the copy*); the remedy is span-or-size in **T85's order** — unruled tracking out first, re-measure, and only then T74. **Correction — the gate's failure message prescribes a remedy canon forbids:** it reads *"if both miss, the budget itself is wrong and the deck needs a third line, not a truncation"*, and a third line is RE-AUTHORING, which G1-am ruled is the last resort and only after the span route fails. The gate predates that ruling. **A gate's failure text is read as an instruction and must not prescribe a remedy the owning document forbids** — corrected in the same commit as the fix. Register-level observation; promotes to a law on a second catch **G1-am4 · batch 40 — the tabular font is a NEW GEOMETRY EVENT and it lands exactly on the row G1-am3 left provisional.** G1-am3 recorded the compact column's PASS as provisional against this precise change, and it has now happened: **T82's derived font changes what a digit measures.** `UNDER 10.5 CORNERS` and `UNDER 10.5 CNRS` passed 143 px **with room to spare against PROPORTIONAL digits**, and a tabular set is normalised toward the widest digit, so tabular figures are typically wider than the proportional `1` they replace — **that room is precisely what changed.** **Ruled: the compact column's DIGIT-BEARING rows re-certify against the tree carrying the derived font** (C18 §4.1, the fourth geometry event on this one certification); the digit-free rows are final and are not re-read. **The NEED column's failure is untouched** — every miss there is digit-free, which is exactly why G1-am3 recorded it as final: the tabular font can neither rescue nor worsen it. **Consequence for T84's sweep, stated because it is running:** on a tree already carrying the derived font it catches this for free; if it ran BEFORE the font landed, **its digit-bearing rows are void and re-run** — not wrong, merely certifying a geometry that no longer exists **G1-am5 · batch 60 — THE DIGIT-BEARING COMPACT ROWS RE-CERTIFY ON THE WIRED TREE. FIT ONLY, AND THE SCOPE IS THE POINT.** G1-am4 made the compact column's digit-bearing rows provisional against a FOURTH geometry event, the derived tabular font; that tree is now wired (T82-cl, batch 58) and shot. **Re-certified on frames, both after-sets, five seeds:** `MUSKRATS ML −280`, `OVER 2.5 GOALS −227`, `PAVEMENT ANYTIME +459`, `AUDITORS ML −272`, `LANYARD ANYTIME +245`, `TURNIPS ML −156`, `KETCHUP ANYTIME +184`, `BTTS NO −176` — **every one renders complete inside its column.** **The fourth geometry event on this one certification is DISCHARGED**, and with G1-am3's figure-free rows already final **the whole compact deck is now final.** The NEED column's failure is untouched and still final for G1-am3's own reason — every miss there is digit-free, so the tabular font could neither rescue nor worsen it. **SCOPED HONESTLY, and this is the half that matters: THIS CERTIFIES FIT, NOT CLEARANCE.** The gap between the price and the state word **beside these same figures** has gone to zero (**T91**), and the two must not be read as one verdict — **a row whose every member fits is not a row that reads** **G1-am6 · batch 61 — THE MONEYLINE NEED ARM: AUTHORING REOPENS, on G1-am's OWN STATED CONDITION.** **`SPREADSHEETS TO WIN` is 289.9px against a 265.0px column — 24.9px past the column's FULL width**; no span inside the locked column carries this arm, T90-am's padding levers buying 16px against a 24.9px need. **G1-am's condition fires exactly as written** — *authoring reopens ONLY if a string overruns AND the span route cannot carry it, a finding the after-set produces, never an assumption to build on now.* **The after-set produced it; the span route demonstrably cannot carry it. AUTHORING REOPENS FOR THE MONEYLINE NEED ARM ONLY** — nothing else in the deck is affected and **the compact deck is untouched and still final (G1-am5).** **CREDITED, and it is the most valuable thing in the submission: TV flagged this BEFORE the capture window rather than after.** A residual found after the window costs a whole capture and a re-read; found before it, it costs one authoring answer — **C17's economics running forwards.** **THE AUTHORING, and the one fact that decides it:** the deck already carries a complete short form, **`TO WIN`**, authored so truncation is never reached — the question is whether dropping the club word loses a fact the player needs. **On the frames it is genuinely ambiguous, so it is not being guessed:** the NEED line carries the LIVE leg's identity while the compact rows carry the others, so `TO WIN` alone would leave the live leg's side unnamed — **unless the surface already marks it**, and the scorebug renders a dot before one team name (`● MIDDLEMEN 1 — 0 STARTUPS`), which is **T23's backed-player locator and T42's ruled form-not-colour discriminator.** **OWED — ONE FACT: does the scorebug's marker identify the BACKED side of the displayed fixture, and is the NEED line's leg always on the fixture the scorebug is showing?** **DISPOSITIONS PRE-COMMITTED so this costs one answer and no round trip: (1) YES to both → `TO WIN` IS the authored form and NOTHING NEW IS WRITTEN** — the club is named once, by the marker, which is **T69/T70's own ruling** (*the same fact named twice* — the club-qualified form is then the duplicate, not the bare one); **selection is BY MEASUREMENT, NEVER BY THE BACKSTOP** — where the club-qualified form does not fit its box the authored bare form renders instead, which is what G1's fallbacks were authored complete FOR. **(2) NO to either — the NEED leg can sit on a fixture the scorebug is not showing, or the marker means possession → the club MUST be named in the NEED line**, and this seat authors a shorter club rendering against the pool's longest word: **send the pool and it is authored on sight.** **Refused in both branches: truncation as a selector, abbreviation of a money figure (C49), and any change to the compact deck** **G1-am7 · batch 62 — THE MONEYLINE NEED ARM, AUTHORED. A TWO-RUNG LADDER, SELECTED BY MEASUREMENT.** **The owed fact answered: FIRST HALF YES** — `awayMark`/`homeMark` render **only on moneyline legs**, exactly this arm, marking the **picked** side (T23's locator, T42's form-not-colour discriminator, already built). **SECOND HALF NO, with the mechanism named at the line** — `TvSweatScreen.cs:1652–1653` advances the column's live row to leg **N+1** the instant leg N resolves while the scorebug holds leg **N**'s fixture until the next leg stages, and **the window is not a frame**: it spans the whole won-leg or dead-leg beat with the column on screen throughout. **So bare `TO WIN` is UNSAFE exactly as disposition 2 pre-committed** — it would name no side, and the marker it leans on points at a different fixture. **Disposition 2 fires: the club must be named.** **RULED — TWO RUNGS, the surface choosing by MEASUREMENT and never by truncation (`FitOrFallback`, already the mechanism): rung 1 `{CLUB} TO WIN`** (fits 261.0 — **15 of 20**); **rung 2 `{CLUB} WIN`** (the other five). **Bare `TO WIN` is RETIRED as this arm's fallback and must not be reachable on a moneyline leg** — it was the cheap answer and disposition 2 is precisely the finding that it is unavailable. **WHY `{CLUB} WIN` AND NOTHING SHORTER — every alternative that fits by touching the club fails a standing rule: ABBREVIATING THE NOUN IS REFUSED** (a coined short form; T88-am's *no abbreviation coined* and T84-am4's state-chip ruling — and **the noun is ALREADY the club's short form**, the compact rows printing `MUSKRATS ML` not `Tulsa Muskrats`, so there is nothing left to shorten without inventing); **`HOME`/`AWAY` REFUSED** — it binds to the fixture on screen and **fails in exactly the window that made it necessary**; **`{CLUB} ML` REFUSED** — a market label is not a requirement, and the NEED line states what must happen; **wrapping to a second line REFUSED** — the band's geometry was settled one batch ago at T90-am and re-deriving it to buy a string is what §3.5 exists to stop. **AND IT IS THE SLOT'S OWN REGISTER, NOT A NEW ONE:** the NEED deck is already terse declarative — `ONE TEAM BLANKED`, `ONE TEAM SCORELESS` being **subject + required state**, no infinitive and no verb *to be* — **so `SPREADSHEETS WIN` is that same shape**, grammatical because **every noun in the pool is plural**. The one risk worth checking, *could it read as a result rather than a requirement*, **is answered by the slot itself**: `ONE TEAM BLANKED` has carried the identical risk since G1 and reads correctly, because the NEED slot only ever states requirements and the progress line directly beneath carries the live state (`LEVEL 0–0`, `LEADING 1–0`). **THE MEASUREMENT IS NOT ASSERTED HERE (§2.5, C41):** this seat has not measured rung 2 — from the pool's own figures the club-plus-space budget is **167.3px** (261.0 − 93.7) against a worst club of **196.2px**, so rung 2's worst case lands **near 247px on the arithmetic, a DIRECTION OF TRAVEL and not a number to land on**, with the residual against 261.0 looking comfortable rather than marginal. **OWED: `{CLUB} WIN` measured for all twenty against 261.0 — seconds, not a window.** **PRE-COMMITTED: (1) all twenty fit → the ladder is FINAL, the window fires, nothing returns here; (2) any club still overruns → it returns with the widths and this seat authors rung 3, and THE REMEDY WILL BE THE PHRASE, NEVER THE CLUB**, abbreviation of the noun staying refused in every branch. **THE WINDOW — FIRE IT, AS ONE PASS: no separate build-verification capture**, all five fixes being built and a verification window that is not the closing window spending a capture to learn what the closing capture reports anyway. **TV's decision to HOLD was CORRECT and is endorsed** — running against a string known in advance to fail the gate would have produced frames that could not close the phase and guaranteed a second window; **a lane that declines to spend a window on a foreseeable failure is doing the job the capture budget exists for**, and it routed the call rather than making it alone **G1-am8 · batch 63 — THE SCORER ARM: RUNG 2 AUTHORED, `{SURNAME} SCORES`.** TV routed this rather than shipping it and **it is the same defect one arm over**: the scorer arm's rung 2 is the bare form **`TO SCORE`**, which **names no player** — the exact property G1-am7 retired bare `TO WIN` for one batch ago, for T94's reason. **AND IT IS WORSE HERE, on this surface's own evidence: the backed-side marker renders ONLY on moneyline legs** (`isMl && …`, TV's own answer at G1-am6), **so a scorer leg has NO marker at all** and nothing else on the surface names the player. **G1's original ruling makes it decisive** — the AnytimeScorer pair-defect was fixed by having the progress line read `NOT YET`/`SCORED` **precisely because the surname is named ONCE, by the NEED line above it**; retire the surname from the NEED line and **it is named NOWHERE.** **RULED — the same two-rung ladder chosen by measurement: rung 1 `{SURNAME} TO SCORE`, rung 2 `{SURNAME} SCORES`; bare `TO SCORE` is RETIRED as this arm's fallback** and must not be reachable on a scorer leg. **THE RUNG-2 RULE GENERALISES ACROSS BOTH ARMS AND IS STATED ONCE: DROP THE INFINITIVE MARKER AND CONJUGATE TO THE SUBJECT** — clubs are plural and take `WIN`, a surname is singular and takes `SCORES`. **One rule, two arms, no new vocabulary**, and it keeps the slot's established terse-declarative register (`ONE TEAM BLANKED`, `ONE TEAM SCORELESS` being subject + required state). **It also sits correctly against the progress line already ruled:** `PAVEMENT SCORES` above `NOT YET`/`SCORED` reads as requirement-then-state, which is what the pair is for. **NOT MEASURED AT THIS SEAT (§2.5, C41):** `PAVEMENT TO SCORE` is TV's measured **264.9 against 261.0**, over by 3.9, and dropping `TO ` while adding the conjugating `S` **should return more than that on the arithmetic** — **a direction of travel, not a number to land on.** **OWED: `{SURNAME} SCORES` measured across all twelve surnames against 261.0 — seconds.** **PRE-COMMITTED: (1) all twelve fit → the ladder is final, nothing returns here; (2) any surname still overruns → it returns with the widths and this seat authors rung 3, and THE REMEDY WILL BE THE PHRASE, NEVER THE SURNAME** — abbreviating a player's name is refused in every branch, the coined-short-form class (T88-am, T84-am4) applied to **the one string a player is most entitled to see in full.** **DOES NOT GATE PHASE T, and TV's reasoning for saying so is ADOPTED: the gate is that the truncation backstop does not fire, and an authored fallback rendering complete is the ladder WORKING** — T89-A's own example, `ONE TEAM BLANKED`, is exactly that. **A fallback that renders complete but names the wrong amount of the world is a VOICE defect, not a FIT defect**, which is why it is ruled here and not at the gate. **ONE HONEST NOTE: rung 2 of NEITHER arm has been seen rendered** — the seed's ticket carries `MUSKRATS` and `PAVEMENT`, so `{CLUB} WIN` and `{SURNAME} SCORES` appear in no frame the studio holds. **Their FIT is computed, which T84-am2 expressly permits** (*the screening may be computed; the boxes it flags take rendered confirmation*), **and their VOICE is this seat's own authorship rather than a ratification of someone's description** (T88's standard). **Their rendered read is owed at the next capture carrying a long club or a long surname — a note, not a condition, and not a reason to hold a phase whose gate is fit** **G1-am9 · batch 64 — RUNG 2 IS RENDERED, AND RATIFIED AT REVIEW DISTANCE. G1-am8's honest note CLOSES.** That capture is `tv-t95-transitions-2026-08-13/` and it carries a long club. **Seed `30941771` renders `REGULATORS WIN`** — `REGULATORS TO WIN` measured **264.1 against the 261.0 box**, one of the five that overrun, **so rung 2 fired exactly as the ladder specifies** — while the same set renders `MUSKRATS TO WIN` and `STARTUPS TO WIN` at rung 1. **BOTH RUNGS OBSERVED IN ONE SET: the selector picks by MEASUREMENT, on frames.** **THE VOICE CALL, made against the RENDERED STRING rather than a description of it (T88's standard): `REGULATORS WIN` reads as a REQUIREMENT, not a result. RATIFIED.** The disambiguation is exactly what G1-am7 argued and **stronger on the frame than in the argument**: the line beneath reads **`TRAILING 0–1`** and the scoreline above reads **`PLUMBERS 1 — 0 REGULATORS`**, so **a result-reading is not merely unlikely, it is CONTRADICTED TWICE ON THE SAME SCREEN** — *Regulators win* cannot be read as an outcome while the surface says they are trailing and losing 1–0. **The pair carries the tense, which is what the pair is for.** **Still owed and unchanged: `{SURNAME} SCORES` measured across the twelve surnames, and its rendered read at a capture carrying a long surname** — the scorer arm's rung 1 is visible here and complete (`MUFFIN ANYTIME`, `RACKET ANYTIME`, `PAVEMENT ANYTIME` in compact), rung 2 is not |
| V6 | The room-region gate reads a different quantity than its bounds describe | **RULED — room's recommendation ADOPTED: V6 measures in CIELAB via the room's shared `linear_to_lab`; the bounds stay exactly as ruled, 85–92°** · DD 2026-08-11 batch 37, on room's provenance trace (`6e61c9d`). New row — V6 has been referenced from T65 since batch 13 and never had one. Provenance ACCEPTED in full: traced three independent ways (vocabulary — the room pairs hue with CHROMA, never *saturation*; instrument — the lane's only hue tool is `linear_to_lab`, and the single HSV hit in room code is `v6_room_region.py` itself, added by TV at `97350ae`; computation — CIELAB lands within 1.9° on the lid and 10.7° on the key while HSV misses by 39–51°). **Why the READING moves and not the bounds:** the bounds ARE the studio's palette figures (lid 85.1–85.3°, warm key ~92°, both entering at batch 13 as this lane's CIELAB measurements); re-deriving them in HSV would keep the code but orphan the numbers T65 clause 4 cites and leave every room palette figure in the studio permanently incomparable with V6's output — under C33-am3 (*the three ladders are never compared*) a gate whose output cannot be compared with the palette it polices is a STANDING defect, not a local one. **Naming the space is necessary and NOT sufficient — do both.** Room is exactly right that a label makes the mismatch visible without making the bounds correct; the asymmetry inside the file is the tell (line 91 names its luma unit per C33, the hue line names nothing) and **that asymmetry is where the error entered**, so leaving it after a conversion invites the next one. **EVERY historical V6 verdict is VOID — BOTH directions.** Room's self-test settles it: feed the band the two colours it was derived from and **both fail it** — a gate whose own reference points fall outside its own band is not mis-calibrated, it is reading a different quantity. The regions are **DISJOINT, not shifted** (HSV 85–92° ≈ CIELAB 124–128°, green), so in-band verdicts certify nothing AND out-of-band verdicts are equally void — a rejected value may have been a correct warm one. TV's "every in-band verdict is suspect" is right in direction and understates it. **This recovers work as often as it discards it** (C37's shape, and why the ruling is *void* rather than *failed*). **TV OWES an inventory of V6 verdicts already acted on, both directions** (C18 §4.1 — an inventory names its members); room correctly disclaims this as TV's record, and "we'll re-run it" is not the deliverable, the members are. **Ownership (C20, both lanes present):** `v6_room_region.py` is TV's, `linear_to_lab` is room's and already shared by the emission instrument and the R23 cast — **the converter is SHARED, NEVER FORKED**, because a second implementation is exactly how a two-space defect regrows and it would be invisible to the same self-test that caught this one. **The amplitude window is NOT independent evidence:** `[0.78, 1.06]` and the *"130° at zero falling to ~45.5°"* trajectory sit in the same source comment as the HSV 88.0° and are functions of the band — **both VOID as evidence, re-derived against the converted gate**; they bound nothing today and anyone citing them is citing an HSV derivation of a CIELAB constraint. **NOT FROZEN, and that is the useful sequencing:** T80-am froze T65's re-authored VALUE because it moves rendered pixels inside the Phase T pair, but V6 is an INSTRUMENT — converting its reading changes no rendered frame, so no freeze attaches. **Repair the gate during the freeze**, so that when the freeze lifts T65's value is judged by an instrument that works; lifting the freeze first and measuring against the broken gate spends the after-set on a verdict void on arrival | batch 37 |

| T87-am | The scoreless draw — the three pre-committed dispositions, ruled on frames | **RULED on `dd-import/tv-goalless-draw-2026-08-14/` (120 frames, both tickets, one settlement)** — DD 2026-08-14 batch 66. **Disposition (1) fires on the BEAT; it does not close the ROW.** The ending is legible as a resolution in both halves — draw-backer: `LEG 1 — WON` from frame 000, room **+7.64 mean lift across 76.7% of room pixels** (onset f016, peak f017, rest ~f037), gold flood, tally, confetti; team-backer: `LEG 1 — DEAD`, **no added light at any frame**. **Neither half can be mistaken for idle — the check passes on frames.** **Disposition (2) DOES NOT FIRE** (the ending is not idle) — **but its remedy is owed for a different reason**: §6.8 assigns the event strip the job of stating the fact and **across 120 frames it never does**, carrying the LEG's grade instead of the MATCH's ending; the ending is currently carried by the settlement machinery and the scorebug's `FT` alone. **OWED: the strip's full-time statement at its L2 tier (T66) — never a flourish.** **Disposition (3) DOES NOT FIRE — structurally impossible**: `WinBeat()` is one path for every winning ticket (`EmissionFlash(goldL4)`, `RoomSettlementGlow()`, `PunchThenSettle`, `WinConfetti()`, tally off `PotentialPayout`) and **reads no market kind, goal count or scoreline**; the frames show every element firing. **T87 STANDS UNAMENDED.** Set limit recorded: tally/confetti run 2.0s against a 1.2 sim-second capture, so at frame 059 the slot reads `+$63` against `PAYS $86` — **the set ends mid-tally and no claim is made about how the beat RESTS** | batch 66 |
| T96 | The draw leg prints as a team bet | **NEW — the worst thing on the frames, and an UNIMPLEMENTED ruling rather than a new one** — DD 2026-08-14 batch 66. On the same matchup at full time: **`TICKET 1/2  MIDDLEMEN ML  +243  W`** (the DRAW ticket) and **`TICKET 2/2  MIDDLEMEN ML  +132  L`** — **two tickets, the same label, opposite grades.** The winning row tells the player he backed Middlemen to win, on a match Middlemen did not win, and marks it WON. **The grading is CORRECT** (harness places `MarketSelection.MoneylineDraw()`; C47 holds exactly as ruled) — **the defect is purely presentational, which is why it survived a passing capture.** Cause: `TvSweatScreen.LegStatement()` case `MarketKind.Moneyline` is a two-way `pickedHome ? Home : Away` → `$"{club} ML"`, **with no third branch**, so a `MarketChoice.Draw` leg is named after whichever club the bool lands on. **`tv-design.md` §8 already carries the fix and names the failing string verbatim**: the draw's compact form is **`DRAW`** (S74), and *"the moneyline's other forms assume a team (`MIDDLEMEN ML`) and a draw leg has none."* **Severity: this misprints on the one player whose ticket just came in** — a nearer miss than T87's own named worst case, because his result is not absent, it is **attributed to a bet he did not place** | batch 66 |
| T97 | The strip carried a goal into a goalless full time | **NEW — C50's shape** — DD 2026-08-14 batch 66. `scene002` frames 000–030 — **31 of 60, more than half the ending** — carry **"Mallards on the board; the slip flinches."** while **the scorebug in the same frame reads `MALLARDS 0 — 0 MIDDLEMEN · FT`** (one distinct scorebug state across all 60, measured). The line is `game-console/EventText.cs:111`, a member of the **`ScoreDown` array — the opponent-scored family** — so it is **a goal line by construction, on a full time with no goals**. **The harness cannot catch it**: it asserts plumbing, and the score and clock it does assert are both correct. **Breaches §8 directly** — the strip *"never duplicates the score"*; **this does not duplicate it, it contradicts it**, which §8 never needed to forbid. **RULED a defect. The strip must not carry a goal line into a state with no goal.** Whether the line is stale carry-over or freshly selected is the lead's diagnosis to report — **both causes are defects and neither changes the ruling.** **Land with T87-am's owed L2 full-time statement — one slot, one repair** | batch 66 |
| T65-am | The settlement capture arrived and does NOT close the hue item | **CORRECTED — the open row said *"owed a settlement capture"*; the capture is here and the row stays open, because a full-room capture is the WRONG INSTRUMENT** — DD 2026-08-14 batch 66. Measured on `scene001`, room pixels only: peak f017 **+7.64 mean lift, 76.7% of room pixels, hue 24.9°**, decaying 31.4° (f020) → 44.2° (f025) → 60.4° (f030) → rest. Authored `roomSettlementWarm` = (0.818, 1.000, 0.610) = **hue 88.0°** at intensity 0.9, acceptance band **85–92°**. **The measured hue cannot be compared to it because it TRACKS DISTANCE FROM THE PANEL**: at f017, near-TV **19.1°**, far edge **24.8°**, far corner **31.5°** (at f025: 36.6 / 46.4 / 69.6). **A single light cannot cast three hues at three distances** — `EmissionFlash(goldL4)` fires on the same frame in `WinBeat()` and **its spill is the confound.** **RULED: closed by V6's printed hue / the R23 `RoomViewCapture` path (which reads `roomSettlementWarm` directly), never by another in-room settlement capture, however good.** **What this set DOES settle: the glow fires on a goalless settlement, at a measurable magnitude with a clean decay to rest** — the T87 half, closed | batch 66 |

| T96-am | The draw leg's compact form — RULED AND FIXED IN THIS BATCH; the DECK was the defect | **RULED — and the blame in batch 66 was wrong: the BUILD IS FAITHFUL** — DD 2026-08-14 batch 68. `LegStatement()`'s two-way Moneyline branch is **exactly what the copy deck told it to build**: `tv-g1-authored-leg-statements-2026-08-08.md` carried a single `| Moneyline | {CLUB} TO WIN | {CLUB} ML |` row and **ZERO occurrences of the word "draw"**. **The deck was written 2026-08-08; S74 authored the draw's forms 2026-08-12; the deck was never amended** — so **§8's claim that the draw's forms *"live with the rest"* was FALSE against the very file §8 points builders at.** **The deck is a DD-owned file, so this batch AMENDS it rather than filing a request**: `Moneyline · Home/Away` and **`Moneyline · Draw` → NEED `LEVEL AT FULL TIME`, compact `DRAW`**, progress `LEVEL` / `NOT LEVEL` (S74, unchanged), with a fallback row (`LEVEL AT FULL TIME` is 18 at the NEED budget, same class as `ONE TEAM SCORELESS`; authored shorter line **`LEVEL AT FT`**, `FT` being **this surface's own clock token**, not jargon) and an explicit **no fallback needed for `DRAW`** at 4 chars. **THE REUSABLE HALF: a copy ruling lands in the DECK or it has not landed** — S74 was ruled, folded into the owning doc, and still shipped a defect, because the deck sat between the doc and the build | batch 68 |
| T97-am | The strip's words are licensed by the RESOLVED SCENE, never the beat's type — and the remedy already existed | **RULED, and the fix is an EXISTING guard applied to a second family** — DD 2026-08-14 batch 68. Mechanism: `RenderEvent` stashes `_pendingFlavor`, `RevealBeatChrome` lands it into `_tFlavor` (**the strip — confirmed at `AtTier(ink, TierL2)`, T66's own tier**); the line comes from `SweatFlavor.Line` → `Base(e.Type, …)`, **selected by the beat's TYPE**, so a beat typed `Score` prints a goal sentence **whether or not the resolved scene contains a goal**. **`SweatFlavor.NeutralLine` ALREADY EXISTS for exactly this defect one market family over** — its own comment reads *"a count-market beat whose resolved scene carries no count event … corner/booking words would be a lie there"* (Sol, F_0.4.0 P3 r2) — **and the orchestrator already overrides with it for counts. The goal families never got the same override.** **THE LAW, stated generally because this is its second instance: a beat's WORDS are licensed by what the resolved scene actually contains, never by the beat's type label alone** — where the scene lacks the asserted event, the authored neutral line stands in. **Scope: every family that ASSERTS A GOAL** — `ScoreUp`, `ScoreDown`, and the finishing members of `BigUp`/`BigDown`; **the lead sweeps those four arrays string by string** and reports which assert a goal versus only a dangerous move (**this seat did not audit every line**). **The `NearMiss` overrides are already correct and are the model.** **Stale-carry vs fresh mis-selection is the lead's diagnosis to report — both are the same defect under the law and neither changes the remedy** | batch 68 |
| T87-am2 | The owed L2 full-time statement — AUTHORED. Batch 66's owed item DISCHARGED | **AUTHORED: `THE MATCH ENDS LEVEL`** — DD 2026-08-14 batch 68. Fires at the whistle of a **drawn** match into the event strip at L2 (T66), holding until the leg's grade displaces it; **the existing window is not shortened to make room.** **`FULL TIME — LEVEL` was the obvious form and is REFUSED**: the scorebug prints `FT` one slot above, and **stating the same fact twice one slot apart is §8's duplication rule with a different neighbour** — the strip's job is to say what the score and clock cannot. Takes the shape AND casing of the surface's own beat statements (`THE BOARD IS SET`, `THE TOTEM BURNS`) — **every authored line in `_tFlavor` is caps**; the sentence-case lines come from the `EventText` path alone. `LEVEL` is this surface's word for a tied scoreline (T62, S74) — nothing invented. **It is the DRAWN match's line, not the goalless one**: true at 0–0 and 2–2 alike, and **a goalless-only line would be exactly the narrowing §6.8 forbids**, making 0–0 a special case again. **Why only a draw needs one, and it is structural: a decided match ends ON a goal so its final beat's line IS its ending — a drawn match ends on nothing, so the last beat's line is stale by construction.** **The proof the statement is absent rather than played before the capture: `scene002` gives 31 frames of strip between the whistle and its leg grade, and no full-time statement appears in them** — the window exists, is long enough, and is occupied by T97's falsehood | batch 68 |
| T70-am | "No term repeated across the two" governs the SUBJECT, not the predicate | **RULED — no breach by S74's pair** — DD 2026-08-14 batch 68, raised because amending the deck forced it. NEED `LEVEL AT FULL TIME` over progress `LEVEL` repeats `LEVEL`, and T70 reads *no term repeated across the two*. **T70 governs redundant IDENTIFICATION**: its own example is `LANYARD TO SCORE` over `WAITING FOR LANYARD` — **a name printed twice** — and T70 calls it *"T69's defect turned vertical"*, where T69 is **the backed team printed twice**. Both are the subject. **A binary state answering its own requirement in the requirement's own word is the progress line doing its only job**, not redundant identification. **Forcing a different word below would put a second name on one thing and break the one-name-per-thing convention T62 established — the cure would be the worse defect** | batch 68 |

| T96-vf | The draw leg's compact form — VERIFIED ON FRAMES | **DESIGN-VERIFIED** — DD 2026-08-14 batch 69, on the reshot set. `TICKET 1/2 · **DRAW** · +243 · W` and `TICKET 2/2 · MIDDLEMEN ML · +132 · L` — **the two rows no longer print one string with opposite grades, and the Home/Away branch did not regress.** Settlement treatment unchanged (gold flood, tally `+$3` → `+$63`, confetti, room glow). **ONE CLAUSE NOT VERIFIABLE FROM THIS SET and the README asserts it anyway**: the live **NEED** `LEVEL AT FULL TIME` over progress `LEVEL` — **both tickets are settled in all 120 frames**, so the live row is never in shot. **Not a defect; a claim without a frame (C11). Owed: any mid-match frame of a draw-backed leg** | batch 69 |
| T97-f1 | The goal-family guard — FAILS ON FRAMES, and the build is not the excuse | **FAILS** — DD 2026-08-14 batch 69. **The strip is unchanged in behaviour from the pre-ruling set**: `scene002` still shows **2 states at frames [0, 31]** — `Mallards on the board; the slip flinches.` over a `0 — 0 · FT` scorebug for **31 of 60 frames** — and `scene001` still shows 1 state, `LEG 1 — WON`. **The fix is present and correctly written** (`:1528-1531` guards `Score` and `BigPlay`, excludes `NearMiss`, gates on `!spec.Goal.HasValue`, routes to `NoGoalLine`, which keeps the danger-only `BigPlay` members and otherwise falls to `NeutralLine` — the ruling, implemented as ruled). **AND THE BUILD CONTAINED IT**: `DRAW`, `THE MATCH ENDS LEVEL` and `BigUpAssertsGoal` all entered in one commit (`1be8140`) and **`DRAW` renders on these frames** — so the strip code was compiled and live and still did not reach the screen. **A stale build would have failed all three; this failed exactly the two that share a slot.** **HYPOTHESIS, not a frame claim: `_pendingFlavor` has several writers and the guarded one is not last** — `:1531` writes it, then `RenderEvent` writes `_pendingFlavor = flavor` **unguarded** at `:2821`. **Diagnostic that settles it: log every write to `_tFlavor.text` / `_pendingFlavor` with its call site across one `LegFinal` beat** | batch 69 |
| T87-am3 | The drawn match's line — FAILS ON FRAMES, **and the ruling was under-specified; that half is the DD seat's** | **FAILS, with the missing clause supplied** — DD 2026-08-14 batch 69. `THE MATCH ENDS LEVEL` appears in **none of the 120 frames**, though `:2803` sets it correctly on `LegFinal && Picked == Opponent`; **the leg's grade writes `_tFlavor.text` directly at `:3268`/`:3304` and the frames say the grade wins.** **THE SEAT'S OWN ERROR: batch 68 ruled the line *"holds until the leg's grade displaces it"*, which ASSUMED A WINDOW EXISTS — on a won draw-backer it does not.** `scene001` shows `LEG 1 — WON` already up at **frame 000, the whistle itself**: **the match ending and the leg's resolution are the same instant**, so a line that yields to the grade yields before it is ever seen. **The ruling described a sequence the beat model does not provide.** **RULED NOW: the line takes a MINIMUM HOLD before the grade may displace it; the grade NEVER lands on the same frame as the line** (a statement replaced on its own entrance frame was never made); **the hold is a NAMED constant, not a literal**, taking `ticketDeadConsolationDuration = 1.0f` as its precedent — **match it rather than invent a number**. **There is room**: `scene002` already shows **31 frames / 0.62 sim-seconds of dead window**. **Verifiable by construction — the line must be VISIBLE, for multiple frames, BEFORE the grade appears.** **General lesson: a ruling that authors copy into a SHARED slot must say which statement yields and for how long** | batch 69 |

| T96/T97/T87-am2 · CLOSED | The three rulings — ALL DESIGN-VERIFIED on the reshot set | **DESIGN-VERIFIED** — DD 2026-08-15 batch 70, measured at this seat on 128 frames (120 endings + 8 mid-match), **with this seat's own instrument rather than the harness log**. **T87-am2:** `THE MATCH ENDS LEVEL` frames **000–050**, `LEG 1 — WON` at **051** — **51 frames = 1.02 sim-seconds, matching the ruled `drawnEndingHoldDuration = 1.0f`**, and never the same frame as the grade. **T97:** no `ScoreUp`/`ScoreDown`/`BigPlay` sentence in any of the 128. **T96:** the mid-match shot carries the live **NEED `LEVEL AT FULL TIME`** over progress **`LEVEL`** on a `0 — 0` at `30'` — the clause the previous README asserted while every frame was settled. **Coverage note, not a defect:** `scene003` holds the line all 60 frames (its grade beat falls outside the 1.2s window), so **`LEG 1 — DEAD` is not in this set** — verified on the batch-68 set, that path unchanged, **no supplemental shot required.** **THIS SEAT'S BATCH-69 HYPOTHESIS WAS WRONG** — the offered `_pendingFlavor` race did not exist; `RevealBeatChrome` sits inside `TheaterBeat`'s `evt.Type != LegFinal` branch, so **the stash was simply dropped and the line was never written.** It was offered as a hypothesis with one diagnostic attached, the diagnostic was run, and **that is why being wrong cost nothing.** **The T97 half vindicates the law's wording**: the guard gated on `spec.Goal.HasValue` — the beat's **staged intent** — where the law says the words are licensed by what the resolved scene **CONTAINS**; now gated on `Commits`. **A law written one word loosely would not have caught it** | batch 70 |
| T98 | `— LEAD CHANGE` over a 0–0 — the word comes off, the TAG stays | **RULED against BOTH readings offered** — DD 2026-08-15 batch 70, on 8 of 8 mid-match frames: `Middlemen squeezing the half. — LEAD CHANGE` over `MALLARDS 0 — 0 MIDDLEMEN` at `30'`. **Reading A's diagnosis is WRONG and this is NOT T97 a third time:** `DramaGenerator.cs:116` sets the tag on `(p[i-1] − 0.5) × (p[i] − 0.5) < 0` — **the win probability crossing 0.5, not the scoreline** — so the scene DOES contain the event, and **applying T97's guard would suppress a real fact.** **Reading B's diagnosis is RIGHT and its remedy is BANNED:** §8 rules *the theatre prints facts and offers, not opinions* — **a probability is the house's opinion**, which is why the win-probability numeral went, and **a line announcing the 50% crossing is that deleted numeral's MEANING without its digits.** **RULED: the word is removed** (`SweatFlavor.cs:47`) — not because the event is fake but because **it is the house's opinion, and this surface does not print opinions.** **Nothing he can act on is lost**: the cash-out price already prices off `WinProbAfter`, so the crossing is visible as **the offer moving through its own midpoint** — the offer is this surface's permitted expression of a probability. **THE TAG IS NOT REMOVED** — `TheaterChoreographer.cs:90,117` and `leadChangeMs` use it for staging and pacing, untouched: **a tag may drive timing and staging without earning a word.** **Second defect closed by the same fix:** the line carried **sentence case and uppercase together**, against §8's *one casing*. **If a SCORELINE lead change ever earns a word it is licensed by the scoreline and takes its own tag** — likely never needed, since the scorebug already changes on one frame (T38). **No capture owed** — the absence of a suffix is verifiable on any existing mid-match frame | batch 70 |

| C39 | Enumerate the path before auditing the segment you own | **Law (register-level; deliberately NOT proposed for the constitution — one founding case)** · DD 2026-08-09 batch 20: a complaint's candidate set is the whole path from build to eye; enumerate every segment, check the cheap ones first, audit your own segment LAST. | C40 | A rule states the property it protects; a proxy is labelled as one | **Law** · DD 2026-08-09 batch 28: where a ruling names a detectable test alongside the property it protects, the test is labelled as the PROXY and the property as the LAW — so a passing proxy is never read as the property held (the S71-am2 false positive is the founding case: a word-count proxy read as a voice law) | batch 28 |

| C42 | A confound is separated by an invariant in the same frame, not by a bound on its size | **Law (register-level; deliberately NOT proposed for the constitution — one founding case, C39's precedent)** · DD 2026-08-10 batch 31: a bound is a standing claim about runs nobody has taken (batch 29's needed an n it never had); an in-frame invariant re-establishes itself every measurement. | C43 | A migration moves one variable, because its verification pair is the instrument | **Law (register-level, C39/C42's precedent — promotes on a second catch)** · DD 2026-08-11 batch 32: a migration's before/after pair is only an instrument if the migration is the ONLY moved variable; folding a second change (a size re-derivation, a weight fix) into it destroys the pair's power to certify either. Founding case: T74 — the size-authority conflict must not be settled silently inside Phase T. *(Row moved from the TV table to Cross-surface 2026-08-11 — factual bookkeeping, not an amendment: a cross-surface law transcribed into a surface's table is invisible to anyone reading the laws end-to-end, and C43 is now load-bearing in three items (T74, T79, T80). ID unchanged per C22.1; the ruling is untouched. Section counts move TV 86→85, Cross-surface 41→42; total unchanged at 265.)* **Cause, recorded at the authoring end (§1.5 — this seat's, not the transcriber's):** batch 32 placed C43 as its final section after T72–T78 and closed with the single instruction *"transcribe T72–T78 and C43 into the tables"* — a cross-surface law bundled into a TV-item list with **no destination table named**. Transcribing in that reading order lands it after the T-block, which is what happened; the transcription followed the instruction as written, which is exactly the case §1.5 protects. **Standing practice, at authoring not transcription: every batch names the destination table for every row it ships**, because the authoring seat is where that information exists. Binds this seat too — the DD has transcribed its own rows directly since batch 20 | batch 32 **C43-am · batch 35 — ONE VARIABLE MEANS ONE *SEPARABLE* VARIABLE (first live application, ruled at T81).** C43's named examples — a size re-derivation, a weight fix — are changes that COULD have been sequenced out, and T79 was, on exactly that ground. This adds the test for when they cannot be. **Two changes are ONE variable when neither can be performed without the other. The test is SEPARABILITY, not size:** if arm A cannot be executed without also landing some value of B, B is not a fold — it is a parameter of A, and the pair is honest at the resolution of both. **Three corollaries, all exercised at T81:** (1) **check for a null arm first** — where a migration's target medium REQUIRES a value the source medium supplied implicitly, there is no "leave it alone" arm, only a choice of value; preserving the legacy value is itself a change, usually to a third value matching neither; (2) **a pair whose blind spot cannot be NAMED loses to one whose can** (C18 §4.2) — a change that isolates unevenly across the surface, with no in-frame marker of where it isolated, is a worse instrument than a uniformly coarser pair that states its resolution in one line; (3) **coarsening is bounded at authoring** — a pair ruled coarse names a CLOSED list of what its variable contains, everything else stays sequenced out, and nothing joins the list afterwards. **The precedence C43 does not override:** where a surface's owning document has already ruled the value a migration must land, or has already assigned the migration's pair a question, C43 does NOT license shipping a non-canon set to protect the pair — §1.2 puts the owning document above the register's ruling for the item on that surface's content. **Not promoted to the constitution:** still register-level; this seat does not promote its own law inside the batch that first applies it |

Neither blocks; both owed before either surface is called finished | batch 8 **C26-am3 · batch 20 — the phone's stub expires (phone only; other surfaces stay closed):** the phone and laptop speak in one glance with two voices (lowercase sentence-case in a non-studio face vs uppercase Archivo; ~85% empty; header hue unmeasured against a retired-hue register). The phone's owning doc is OWED, written at the DD seat, sequenced AFTER the phone has a reference frame.
