| C44 | A bound and the reading it judges must come from one instrument | **Law (register-level, C43's standing — not a constitutional amendment)** · DD 2026-08-11 batch 37: **a bound and the reading compared against it must be produced by the same instrument, in the same stated space, and a gate demonstrates this by printing both.** *Founding case:* **V6** — bound in CIELAB, reading in HSV, the two regions **DISJOINT**, and both of the gate's own founding colours failing its own band; every verdict it ever issued was void in both directions and nothing in the pipeline was positioned to notice. *Second and third catches, inside one fortnight:* this seat's own batch-36 T65 target (an authored value ruled against a rendered band — T65-am2), and the room lane's twice-paid region-comparison error, which room names in its own T65 record. **Relation to C33-am3:** that clause requires every measurement to state its space and it governed REVIEW PROSE — **V6 stated nothing and nothing required it to**, because the clause never reached a gate's own output, which is where the comparison actually happens. C44 puts it there. **The cheap test, promoted from room's own method: feed a bound the values it was derived from; if they fail, the gate is reading a different quantity than its bounds describe.** It costs one run, needs no frames, and is the only check in this family that catches a **disjoint** band rather than a merely loose one — a shifted band still passes its own founding values, a disjoint one cannot. **Standing practice: any gate carrying a numeric bound runs its founding values through itself once, and records the result beside the bound** **V6-am · batch 51 — THE BAND'S TOP IS WITHDRAWN, and this seat installed it wrongly (§1.5); V6's BOUNDS are void with it.** Room's R23 pair (`38c44da`) fired **disposition 3**: the anchors do not separate — **the high anchor reads 101.4 against a recorded ~92, a 9.4° miss on a 7° band** — and the subject was correctly **not reported as conforming**. **The find is decisive: two independent computations agree and both disagree with the record** — the key tube's **authored** colour computes **102.7°**, its **rendered** contribution measures **101.4°**, they agree within **1.3°**, and the recorded top carries **no stated derivation at all**. **That is C44's founding shape applied to a BAND rather than a gate: feed a bound its own founding value and it fails.** **§1.5 — THE NUMBER WAS ON THE RECORD AT BATCH 37, INSIDE THE TRACE THIS SEAT ACCEPTED IN FULL.** V6's own row reads *computation — CIELAB lands within 1.9° on the lid and **10.7° on the key***, and **92 + 10.7 = 102.7, exactly the authored figure measured today**. This seat **read a 10.7° miss on a 7°-wide band as CONFIRMATION that the band was correctly drawn** — a miss larger than the band is not agreement, it is the anchor saying it is not there. **Two errors, one cause:** those gaps were also re-labelled *the authored↔rendered gap*, which they never were — they are **record↔computation** gaps, the true authored↔rendered gap on the key being **1.3°**. Both mistakes are the same act, **attributing a discrepancy to the wrong pair of quantities — exactly what C44 was written to prevent, one batch after this seat wrote it.** **THE THREE-BATCH CHAIN, RECORDED STRAIGHT: batch 36's INSTINCT WAS RIGHT and its remedy wrong** — it restated the band to 83–85° reasoning that *the upper half brackets no measured member*, and the upper half indeed bracketed none, **because the real member sits ABOVE the band rather than below its top**; **batch 37 rejected 36's remedy correctly and then installed a provenance that did not exist**, restoring a top the trace in front of it was contradicting. **Batch 36 deserves the credit its correction took away — it detected the defect.** **RULED: the recorded ~92 is WITHDRAWN, and this seat DECLINES to install 101.4 in its place** — because **the choice of the key tube as the upper anchor is itself underived**, having entered the same way ~92 did, and moving the number without deriving the membership **repeats the exact defect one number later**, leaving the register carrying its third unsourced band top in three batches. **THE BAND IS RE-DERIVED ONCE, and the derivation states: (1) every MEMBER NAMED** (C18 §4.1) with its value, its **space** (CIELAB on linear, C33-am3), whether it is **authored or rendered**, and the surface or frame it was taken on; **(2) whether the key tube and the screen emitters are ONE POPULATION AT ALL** — the screens cluster tightly (lid 85.1–85.3, phone 85.4, laptop 84.3, a representative warm emission 83.3) while **the key sits ~16° above all of them**, and **a band spanning a tight cluster plus one distant member is either two populations or a bound so loose it certifies nothing**, which changes every verdict the band will ever issue; **(3) C44's OWN PRACTICE run and recorded beside the bound** — the founding values through the band once — **which had it run when C44 was ruled would have surfaced this immediately, V6 being C44's own founding case**, so the practice was written and not applied to the gate that produced it (recorded as a process finding, not a new law). **DIRECTION OFFERED, EXPLICITLY NOT A RULING:** the settlement re-tint is a **light event**, so the comparison class is the room's warm **LIGHT**, which is the key — the screens being objects lit by it rather than members of the same population; if that holds, a light event is judged against lights and the screen cluster is a different band answering a different question. **The derivation decides this; this seat is naming the fork, not taking it.** **V6's OWN BOUNDS ARE VOID WITH THE TOP:** V6 was repaired by converting its READING to CIELAB while its **bounds stayed 85–92**, so **that repair is incomplete and V6 is still mis-calibrated, now in a new way** — it takes the re-derived band before issuing another verdict; **its converted reading stands, only the bound moves** **V6-am2 · batch 52 — THE BAND, RE-DERIVED. The fork is answered: they are NOT one population and never were.** **Every member named, rendered CIELAB on linear (C33-am3), from the register's own rows:** `ArtIndicator` **49.7°** chroma 5.4 (R41 b16–17, standby lamp under **room-rust law**); `WindowGlow` **77.0°** (R42 b15, **textured emitter — the map governs**); representative warm emission **83.3°** (T65-am); laptop **84.3°**/5.3 and phone **85.4°**/5.0 (R39 b15); laptop lid **85.1–85.3°** (S63-am2 b13); **warm key tube 101.4° rendered / 102.7° authored** (room, `38c44da`). **THE ORIGIN OF ~92 IS NOW VISIBLE IN THE RECORD:** S63-am2, batch 13, reads *room cast 355.7°→85.1° **vs the key's 92°*** — **a passing comparison inside a note about the laptop lid**, never a derivation, never a measurement of the key, never given a space, **which then became a band edge by being cited. That is the whole provenance, and it is why it had none.** **THE FORK, ANSWERED: the spread is 49.7° to 101.4° — 52° wide — which is not a family but FOUR KINDS OF OBJECT measured with one instrument.** **The screens are one family and a tight one** (83.3 / 84.3 / 85.1–85.3 / 85.4, about **2° wide**) and tight **by construction**, R39 having closed them on a **shared base** (`Amp(1/3/15)` off `LaptopScreen.GrantedLidEmission`) — they are unified because one constant makes them so. **`WindowGlow` is a PICTURE, not a palette member** — R42 ruled the authored value a **multiplier** with the **emission map governing**, so its 77.0° is the night-city sodium texture and not a colour anyone chose; **nothing about it is authored as a hue, so it cannot be a band member.** **`ArtIndicator` is ratified rust law** at 49.7°. **And the key tube is the room's LIGHT** — every other member is an object IN the room; the key is what lights them. **RULED: the 83–85° band is the SCREENS' family — correct, well-founded, and the key tube was NEVER a member of it. The phantom top was A LIGHT SOURCE APPENDED TO A FAMILY OF SCREENS**, a category error, and **the same error this lane has now paid for three times in other forms** (authored-vs-rendered, HSV-vs-CIELAB, record-vs-computation) — **comparing across populations is this lane's signature defect and it produced the band's top.** **WHICH POPULATION THE SUBJECT BELONGS TO:** `RoomSettlementGlow()` paints **light into the room** — not a screen, not a texture, not an object — so **its comparison class is the room's warm LIGHT, the key.** **Consequence: the subject was never in the screens' band's jurisdiction at all**, and every verdict judging it against 83–85 or 85–92 was **judging a light event against a family of screens.** **THE BOUND: reference SOURCED, tolerance OWED, and not invented here.** The reference is **the key's rendered 101.4°**, sourced two independent ways agreeing within 1.3° — unlike `~92`. **A one-member population gives a POINT, not a band**, so a tolerance is required and has no derivation yet; **its BASIS is ruled and its NUMBER is not: floor = the instrument's demonstrated spread on this subject**, since a bound tighter than the instrument's own spread cannot fail for what it exists to catch (**C32's founding shape**). **Room reported that spread as *77% of band* — a percentage of a band that is now void — and it is needed IN DEGREES: a restatement of data already in hand, NOT a capture, no window required.** Anything tighter than that floor needs a **perceptual** basis nobody has measured, and **inventing one would be the third unsourced number on this band in four batches** **V6-am3 · batch 53 — THE GATE CHECKS A VALUE AGAINST ITS OWN FIXTURE FAMILY, NOT AGAINST ONE ROOM-WIDE BAND.** A single band across four fixture families was always going to be **either too loose to catch anything or wrong for three of them — and it was both.** **Ruled: V6 checks each value against its own family's bound**, the families being named at T65-cl with their values and provenance; **a value belonging to NO family is not gate-able by a family bound** and is ruled at this seat against the property, which is what T65-cl did. **STANDING, promoted from room's line because it is the general form: A BOUND IS DEFENDED BY THE REASONING THAT GROUPS ITS MEMBERS, NEVER BY HOW TIGHTLY THEY HAPPEN TO CLUSTER.** Clustering is evidence that a grouping **might** exist; it is never the grouping's justification. **Four batches on this band, and every wrong turn — the phantom top, the lights-vs-screens carve, the cross-population comparisons — came from treating PROXIMITY as MEMBERSHIP** | batch 37 |
| C45 | A freeze is checked against the seat's own OUTPUT, not only the lane's input | **Law (register-level, C39/C42/C43's standing — not proposed for the constitution)** · DD 2026-08-12 batch 38: where a freeze protects a verification pair, a routed **measurement** respects it by construction — measurements move no pixels and a lane reporting one is correctly freeze-clean. **The RULING that measurement produces may not be.** The freeze check therefore runs **at the moment of ruling, on what the ruling causes to change**, and **it is this seat's to run because the lane cannot see it**: from inside the lane the submission is freeze-clean and stays that way right up until someone rules on it. **Two founding cases, one batch apart, two different lanes:** **T80-am** — room's T65 settlement-cast measurement, where room stated the freeze was respected and that was true of a measurement and stopped being true when the measurement became a ruling that re-authored a value inside the pair's own frames; and **T82 / T80-am2** — TV's tabular measurement, identical shape, freeze-clean as a finding and a frozen variable the moment option (a) was granted. **Standing practice: any ruling issued while a verification pair is open states, IN THE RULING, whether it creates a frozen variable.** Two independent founding cases is what makes this a law at all; a third argues for promotion to constitution §4 beside the other instrument clauses | batch 38 |
| C46 | A fixed box carries an unstated assumption about the face it was sized against | **Law (register-level, C39/C42/C43's standing — not proposed for the constitution)** · DD 2026-08-12 batch 41, promoted from TV's structural reading of T84's sweep. **Where a layout FIXES a box's width rather than deriving it from content, that box carries an implicit claim — that the longest string it can hold fits, IN THE FACE IT WAS SIZED AGAINST. The claim is never written down, and a change of face, weight, tracking or figure set invalidates every such box at once: silently, and everywhere, because nothing in the box refers to the face it assumed.** Two consequences, both exercised: **(1) sweep the POPULATION, not the suspects** — after any change to type metrics the set at risk is *every fixed box*, not the ones a reader can think of; T84's three founding instances were found by eye in two of nine moments, and the structural reading found the class. **(2) The test is the longest RENDERABLE form, not the current content** — a box passes on what is in it today and fails on the string nobody captured. **Founding case:** Phase T, where a wider canon face produced a truncation (`ONE TEAM BLANKED` to `ONE TEAM`), a collision (`CASH OUT $131OLD E`) and a solo clipping (`MARKET SUSPENDED`) from **the same unstated assumption in three unrelated boxes**. **Retrospective fit, named as retrospective and NOT counted as founding:** S68 on the laptop, the SKIP ROUND headroom collapsing 45 px to 7 px when tracking landed at the wrong value — same shape, a fixed box and a metric change, one surface over. **Relation to the neighbours:** constitution §3.5 says *a bound is not a layout*, obliging the dependent layout to be re-derived; **C46 is its converse and its harder half — a fixed box IS a bound, an implicit one, and nobody re-derives what they never noticed they declared.** R30's *a locked band is not headroom* is the same family and already binds all three surfaces' fixed canvases, which is why this sits cross-surface rather than in TV's table. **Promotes to the constitution on a second INDEPENDENT founding case**; this seat is not counting a retrospective attribution as one **C46-am · batch 56 — A BOX SIZED BEFORE A LAW CANNOT SATISFY IT BY ACCIDENT.** C46 ruled that a fixed box carries an unstated assumption about **the face it was sized against**; the same box carries a second, **about the SET OF OBLIGATIONS its control had when the box was drawn.** **Every correct ruling that adds a required element to a control — a cost, a contract, an instruction, a state — makes that control's box worse**, and four consecutive rulings did it on one surface. **This is not evidence the rulings are wrong; it is the boxes encoding an older contract.** **Consequence, worth having in advance: when a ruling adds an element to a control, the box's fit is RE-DERIVED IN THE SAME BREATH AS THE RULING** — not discovered by a sweep two batches later — and the ruling **states whether it expects the box to still hold, saying so where it cannot tell.** **And the remedy is usually COMPOSITION, not span:** a control that has acquired a fourth obligation is rarely fixed by widening, but by admitting it now has more than one row's worth of things to say — **three instances on one surface in one pass** (the intervention prompt, `TakeoverSub`, the money control), all answered by **N independent strings are not one row** **C46-am2 · batch 58 — THE TWO SWEEP DIRECTIONS ARE COUPLED, and this is TV's finding.** T89-C required both directions on the reasoning that *nothing about the over-generation catch bounds the opposite direction*; **TV found something better — they are not independent.** **An over-generated string is only harmless when it is NOT the maximum. When it IS the maximum it sets the certified worst case, every producible form beneath it is never reached, and it therefore CONCEALS exactly the under-generation the other direction is looking for — so the two directions cannot be swept separately, and finding one does not clear the other.** **Founding case, both halves on one slot:** `BRICKLAYERS ANYTIME` was unproducible **and** the widest member of its set, so removing it did not merely delete a phantom — **it UNCOVERED that both generated arms had been swept against a hand-picked champion**, and that `LegRowNeed0` had been certified **17.5px BETTER than it is** (272.4 → 289.9). **That direction is invisible in frames by construction**, which is why it was the condition this seat cared most about, and **it fired.** **Two further shapes from the same report: the CODE KNEW the worst case and the sweep did not use it** — the construction site's own comment names `GRAVEDIGGERS TO WIN` as over budget, and **a worst case documented in a comment beside the generator is not a worst case the generator uses**; and **a form from the NEIGHBOURING SLOT had been certified against this one's box** (`MIDDLEMEN ML` in `LegRowNeed0`, where `ML` is the LINE slot's vocabulary), so **a worst case must come from the slot's OWN vocabulary, not from a plausible string.** **The remedy is the durable part and is adopted as the standard: CHAMPIONS ARE RETIRED** — the sweep **generates every producible form over the closed pools** (20 club nouns, 12 surnames, both arms each, plus authored constants and both fallbacks verbatim from their construction sites), so **a name added to either pool can no longer be missed by a champion nobody re-picked** — R16's hard-coded list of five materials, one instrument over | batch 41 |
| C47 | The match has three outcomes; a bet has two | **Law, cross-surface** · DD 2026-08-12 batch 49. Sited here rather than in a surface table because it governs **both owning documents' settlement language**, and a cross-surface law transcribed into a surface's table is invisible to anyone reading the laws end-to-end — **C43's founding defect, now seen twice.** **A draw is a third MATCH outcome. It is NOT a third BET outcome. Result language needs no third word: a leg either landed or it did not.** **Ruled: back the draw and it draws — you WON; back a team and it draws — you LOST.** Two words still cover every bet that can be placed, and **S23's enum (PENDING · RIDING · LIVE · GREEN · DEAD · VOID · CASHED OUT) is UNCHANGED and needs nothing added.** **What this prevents, and why it is a law rather than an answer: inventing a `DREW` leg state would be MODELLING THE MATCH INSIDE THE BET'S VOCABULARY** — a category error, and **the same one `Side` already refuses in the engine.** A leg has no opinion about how the match ended; it knows only whether its condition held. **The one legitimate neighbour, named so it is not mistaken for a counter-example:** a market that RETURNS THE STAKE on a draw (draw-no-bet's family) is a **VOID**, which the enum already carries — that is a different market's rule, never a third result. **Laptop echo, ruled by the same line: slip settlement language does not change** — no new word, no new state, no new column | batch 49 |
| C48 | A control's copy is its input contract | **Law, cross-surface** · DD 2026-08-12 batch 50, promoted from T88 and extending T59 to the gesture axis. **Where a control's label names a GESTURE, that gesture is what the input implements — the label is not a description of the control, it is the contract the player acts on.** T59 ruled the availability axis (*display state and input state are the same state, read from one value*); **this is the same law where the input is not merely available or refused but SHAPED** — held, pressed, released. **A label naming a SAFETY PROPERTY the input does not provide is the most dangerous copy a control can carry**, because it is relied upon precisely when the player is being careless, and because the reliance is invisible until the moment it costs him. **Founding case:** the TV's intervention prompt printing `HOLD` over a `wasPressedThisFrame` input that **committed an irreversible spend on the first frame** — no preview, no abandon path, on a surface the player is watching rather than operating (T88). **Corollary, ruled with it: the weight of the gesture matches the weight of the act.** An irreversible spend is never one frame of input; an action that costs nothing and is already the default may be. **The repair direction is fixed and it is the non-obvious half:** where copy and input disagree on a money control, **the INPUT is corrected to match the copy, not the copy to match the input** — correcting the word ships an honest description of an unsafe control, which is worse than either half alone. **Binds all four surfaces**, every one of which has controls whose labels name gestures | batch 50 |
| C49 | Money the player can win or lose is printed in FULL | **Law, cross-surface** · DD 2026-08-13 batch 57, the abbreviation ruling owed at T74-am3. Sited cross-surface because **money precision is a PRODUCT property, not a surface one** — the laptop's ledger, tallies and verdict figures are governed by the same sentence as the theatre's payout. **A money figure the player can win, lose, stake or be paid is printed in full: every digit, no abbreviation, no rounding, no `k`, no `M`, no cap.** **Three refusals, each on its own ground rather than on taste: (1) abbreviation discards precision on the one fact his money depends on, and it does so ONLY FOR LARGE NUMBERS — so it degrades exactly the wins that matter most**; `PAYS $12.3k` is not a payout, it is a description of one. **(2) A format that changes with magnitude is a twitch of a different kind:** this surface has a tabular mandate precisely so figures stop moving as they change, and a rendering that switches form at `$9,999 → $10.0k` **re-introduces the instability from the other end, after T82 paid to remove it.** **(3) A cap is a lie about money** — displaying less than he wins is a false factual claim, and a price or a payout is a fact; **C19's *deliberate cap that prints its own count* governs LISTS, never AMOUNTS.** **Also refused and already ruled elsewhere: the figure does not SHRINK** — `tv-design.md` §8's *copy truncates or chooses a shorter authored line, it never shrinks*, and a content-driven size change is the runtime resize §5.1 and §6 both forbid. **What is left is COMPOSITION and SPAN, which is where every other version of this problem landed today.** **Scope, stated so it is not read wider than it is: this governs AMOUNTS** — not counts, ordinals, clocks or scores — and it does not forbid a non-money aggregate elsewhere choosing a compact form on its own ruling | batch 57 |
| C50 | A pinned run pins the DEAL, not the TIMELINE — frame identity is not beat identity | **Law, cross-surface** · DD 2026-08-13 batch 64. **Third catch, and T83 deferred it to exactly this.** **A claim that two frames show the same moment is made against the moment's OWN OBSERVABLE MARKERS — the score, the clock, the event line, the state — never against the filename, the frame index or the seed.** Pinning a run fixes what is dealt; **it does not fix WHEN anything happens**, so frame N of two runs is not the same beat, and a comparison resting on the index **is comparing two different moments while believing it is comparing one.** **THE THREE CATCHES, on three lanes and in BOTH DIRECTIONS: (1)** R43 one lane over — the seed pins the deal, not the timeline (founding mechanism); **(2) T83** — 21 paired frames matched on moment, seed, scene and index and **differing on grammar**, where the README's *"nothing was substituted"* was true and insufficient — **a FALSE PAIRING**; **(3) T95-cl** — a clean frame at the same index offered as proof of a fix, on a run that had drifted to a different beat — **a FALSE CONFIRMATION.** **T83 wrote that this promotes on a third catch; this is the third**, and it is the first to arrive as a *confirmation* rather than a *comparison*, **which is what completes the shape: the error is SYMMETRIC and both halves are SILENT.** A drifted frame that looks broken invents a defect; a drifted frame that looks clean retires a real one. **Practical form: a set claiming to show a beat states that beat's MARKERS in its own README**, so a reader checks the claim against the artifact rather than against the naming convention | batch 64 |
| C51 | A cross-element invariant is an ASSERTION or it does not exist | **Law, cross-surface** · DD 2026-08-13 batch 64, promoted from T95. **§3.5's enabling instrument.** **Where two elements are required to hold a relationship — the same rect, the same face, the same value, the same state — that requirement is expressed as a MACHINE-CHECKED ASSERTION, or it is not a requirement at all.** A relationship recorded in prose, in a comment beside the code, or in a reviewer's memory **is UNENFORCED**, and the first ruling that moves one element **breaks it silently and everywhere.** **Founding case:** `Score` is the punch overlay and **its own build comment stated the invariant verbatim** — *"Same text, SAME RECT, same face as `_tMatchup` … so superimposing it"* — yet both centre in their own box; T91-am re-bounded one of them and **the comment could not stop the other from staying where it was**, so the scoreline rendered doubled and illegible on every score change until frames caught it. **TV's own line is the clause's short form: A SHARED LOCAL IS A CONVENTION; AN ASSERTION IS A CONTRACT.** **Why this is a LAW and not a note — it is what makes §3.5 COMPLIABLE:** §3.5 obliges the dependent layout to be re-derived in the same commit as the bound that moved, but **nobody can re-derive a dependency they cannot SEE**, and this one was visible only to a reader of one comment in one file. **A rule requiring perfect recall is a rule that will be broken; C51 converts it into one an instrument keeps.** **Second catch of the same shape, named because it is why this promotes now:** C46-am2 found that **the code knew a worst case and the sweep did not use it** — *a worst case documented in a comment beside the generator is not a worst case the generator uses* — **the same defect class on a different instrument: knowledge recorded where nothing can act on it.** **Both instances are on the TV surface**, so this ships as a register-level law binding all four surfaces by its reasoning and **promotes to the constitution on a catch on a SECOND surface**; this seat does not promote to the constitution on one surface's evidence. **Scope, stated so it is not read wider than it is: this governs INVARIANTS BETWEEN ELEMENTS**, not every design decision — **a ruled value lives in the owning document; a required RELATIONSHIP between two elements lives in a test** | batch 64 |

drafts 2–4 are a FAMILY and are never re-authored apart** (the shape is not a template applied to save effort — **the shape IS the claim**, and the variation tracks the meaning). **Sign carried was correct**: reinforcing and opposing are opposite claims about one shared thing. **Draft 1 (`Implies`) NOT APPROVED — it collides with §3.3's existing authored line and drops the reason**: both fire on *one pick contains the other*, both are toner once per slip, so **two code paths would have shipped two sentences for one fact**, and the draft **states the structure but omits the COST**, which is the only part he can act on. **`ScorerSide` withheld — confirmed**, with the one under-determined case pre-committed to the MARK and never to naming the team. **Owed on all seven: the slot was never measured** — the submission measured the stamp forms and not these; **approval is of the copy, fit is not asserted.** **`S79` — the 46.1% silence RULED CORRECT**: a null principal means the price did not move, so **there is nothing to disclose because nothing was taken** — the silence is not an absence of copy but **the absence of a thing to explain**, and a high blank rate is **evidence the model is honest**, since labelling relations it cannot name is the failure S73 forbids. **`S77-am`** — forms comply exactly; **`WIN` supersedes `land` in player-facing copy**; **`NO RUB OUT FIXES THIS SLIP.` NOT APPROVED — a remedy names an ACT**, and a refusal that closes every door is where he most needs the open one named. **13px RATIFIED and the reason is HEADROOM** — this seat's S77 row had quoted 17px from the PLACE label; at 17px the widest form is 295.1px in a 296px box, at 13px it is 76%. **Durable rule: future stamp forms measure at 13px against 296px and stay under 80%, C46's face budget; over 80% is re-authored, never accommodated.** **Plural remedies confirmed real at 6.1% of 41,726 refusals** — S73-am5 was not hypothetical. Full text: `register-entries-2026-08-15-batch-71.md`; folded into `surething-design.md` §3.3 and the same-match amendment

- **Batch 72 (2026-08-15) — written at the DD seat — THE MARK CARRYING THE SIDE: no treatment owed, because the premise was wrong.** su asked for the mark's form to release the held pair; **the honest answer withdraws this seat's own batch-71 disposition (2) rather than building against it.** **The question batch 71 never asked was *under-determined for what purpose?*** — and asking it dissolves the problem: **the statement's job is to explain the PRICE, not to identify the teams**, and *the same team's goals settle both* discharges that completely. **The decisive fact is that HE CHOSE BOTH LEGS** — Biro is the ink for *anything he chose*, so a player cannot be uninformed about which picks he just made. **The mark could not have carried it anyway, and three independent laws refuse it**: the mark is DRAWN not CAPTIONED and already means one thing; S2 bars a side-tinted line; the ink table forbids a fourth ink; a shape variation is a code he must learn. **A pre-commit that survives none of them was not a disposition, it was a guess.** One sub-case weighed and **accepted** rather than left to be rediscovered. **The seven sentences stand as approved, the `ScorerSide` report is withdrawn so su spends no harness run on it, and the held pair is released with no build.** Also recorded: **`T98` verified at the TV seat — the `— LEAD CHANGE` suffix is gone and that row closes.** Full text: `register-entries-2026-08-15-batch-72.md`; folded into `surething-design.md`

- **Batch 73 (2026-08-15) — written at the DD seat — S80, THE RELATION STATEMENT'S PIXELS SPECCED** on Allen's *pay the pixels* ruling. **Four of five parts buildable today.** **The pad survives at 6px** (S51's trade, same answer). **The action band is not the donor and it is arithmetic**: 160 = three ruled control heights (130) + 24px gaps + 6px pad, so absorbing 36 puts `SkipBandY` at −20, below the panel floor. **The donor cited by name under R30 is the MASTHEAD** — 68 → 32, work area 530 → 566, still summing to 704, giving `MarginFlowBudget = 406` = exactly 370 + 36; **rail and tray barred as pixel-identical chrome, tabs cannot yield 36.** **Its virtue: the action stack does not move at all**, which is what T47 is for; **its cost: shared chrome, so every screen pays — the material half, Allen's word.** **THE WARNING that is the reason to spec rather than apply the number: ~36px is the STATEMENT's cost, and the RESERVATION may need ~70.** The cursor chain reconstructs to **374 base, reconciling exactly with S51's measured −374.56 and su's flush-at-370 reading**, so **the base worst case fills the budget with zero slack** — and **the modifiers row is gated on pure RUN state (`OwnsConsumable`), independent of leg count, so it co-occurs**: statement +36, modifiers +34, **both = +70 against a masthead ceiling of 36.** Hence **(a) a possible LATENT overrun today** (4 legs + a held consumable, +34, and S51's pin measures a staged case that appears to exclude modifiers) — **reconstruction, not fact; measure it** — and **(b) if the worst case is 440 the only citable band cannot cover it and the shortfall returns to Allen.** **Owed before the constant is fixed: sweep legs × modifiers × statement and report flow bottom (C46's population, not the suspects).** **The invariant: S51's signed equality retires into `flowBottom ≥ −MarginFlowBudget` plus slack ≤ one leg row, stating its `RectTransform` blind spot.** Full text: `register-entries-2026-08-15-batch-73.md`; folded into `surething-design.md` §2

- **Batch 74 (2026-08-15) — written at a FRESH DD seat — S80's DONOR FAILS, AND S50 ALREADY RULED WHY.** **Process fact first, because it changes what anyone should do next: batch 73 exists on disk, untracked, written 03:22 by the seat that died in the second Orca restart — with its register row and its owning-doc fold.** The restart recovery verified TV's and sgp's disks and did not verify the DD's, so this seat was docketed to spec a move already specced eleven hours earlier. **Nothing lost; ~11h of sequencing was. This batch amends 73 rather than replacing it — both land together and the withdrawn half stays legible.** **THE MASTHEAD CANNOT PAY: its 68px IS its two lines of type** (48 of content between an 8px pad and an 8px clear plus the 2px rule), so **spacing yields ~8px, deleting the round-number subline ~22, a one-line masthead ~26 — 36 is unreachable.** **S50 refused this exact trade on this exact budget twelve days ago** on a deficit 8px larger, citing the locked 34+38+68+530+34=704 in which the masthead's 68 is a term, and took all 44px out of the FLOW instead — **that is the precedent and the yield order it founded.** **R30 is a bar, not a door.** **And Allen already ruled that a Design-verified masthead changes only by closing a conformance gap, never by redesign (2026-08-08, recorded in the masthead's own source).** **THE BILL IS RESTATED AND MEASURED:** the cursor chain sums to 374 and reconciles to a tenth of a pixel against the shipped pin, and the modifiers row is `y -= 34f` on **pure run state**, so **4 legs +0.1 · +consumable +34.1 · +statement +36.1 · +both +70.1** — **a 34px overrun is on the screen today with no statement anywhere near it.** **No gate sees it because the invariant builds one state and its T53 list names the statement's absence but never the consumables axis — an axis nobody wrote down is an axis nobody sweeps.** **Ruled: the blind-spot line takes that axis now; batch 73's sweep becomes the gate on everything rather than on the constant; and Allen gets two bills, not one — his ~36px stands, the other ~34px was never his to price.** **Also shipped: `S74-am2`** — the DRAW row's read PRE-COMMITTED in five checks before the frame docks, the C46 obligation narrowed to two measurements (the cell was sized against `AWAY  -341`, and `DRAW  {odds}` is the same shape), **and the consequence S74 never priced: `MatchupCardPitch` 78 → 116 takes a six-matchup slate from all-six-visible to FOUR — the FORM board stopped being a board he can take in at once, it scrolls so no gate fires, and that is exactly why it needs a ruling.** Full text: `register-entries-2026-08-15-batch-74.md`; `surething-design.md` §2 corrected

- **Batch 75 (2026-08-15) — written at the DD seat — THE DRAW ROW READ: three of five pre-committed checks pass, and the two that fail are one defect with a one-constant fix.** Measured at this seat off the docked flat 1024×704 frame rather than read off the README. **The empty subject cell is LEGAL and the reason is worth keeping: the price column's three words are self-sufficient**, so the draw's line is not a row with a subject-shaped hole — the subject column answers *who is playing* and the answer is two names. **But the draw is not in the middle, and negative space proves it**: the gap above its cell measures **3px** and the gap below **6px**, reconciling to the source's `−8 / −43 / −81`. **Proximity groups AWAY+DRAW against HOME — the opposite of the ruling's own basis that the middle position is literal and attached to neither.** **The cause is an honest slip**: the build moved `HOME` by the TEAM line's pitch (38) when the price column's own pitch was 35, and the two columns have differed since before draws existed — **a third item is what made them disagree on screen.** **Ruled: `DrawOdds` −43f → −44.5f, centred in the span its pinned siblings define; nothing else moves.** **`MORE ›` has stopped straddling** (it now brackets the draw's cell where it used to sit in the 3px gap between two prices) — **accepted, because `MORE` is a column head and takes its scope from the column, and an unexplained nudge would be worse than a coincidence with a reason.** **Also shipped: `S81`, the board's density, measured for Allen and NOT ruled** — the board **never truncated a matchup before this change and now always truncates two**, block 05 rendering a market as a single offer with its draw and home below the fold; **and there is no geometric escape** — six blocks need an 84px pitch and three price cells are 96px before any pad, **so he is choosing between a scrolling board and a shorter slate, never between two layouts.** Full text: `register-entries-2026-08-15-batch-75.md`

- **Batch 76 (2026-08-15) — written at the DD seat — THE SCROLLING BOARD IS ALLEN'S RULING, and the thing it depends on is verified on the frame already in hand.** **`S81` CLOSED by Allen: the scrolling board STAYS — slate holds at six, no pitch change, price face untouched.** All three levers declined. **What he accepted is recorded rather than left implicit: four blocks complete, one partial, one below the fold, and a partial block renders a market as one of its three offers** — batch 75's named hazard, accepted rather than overlooked. **The FORM board is no longer a board the whole slate fits on, by decision.** **`S81-am`: because a permanent scroll is a NEW obligation, S27's position rail was read — nobody had, since until this week it never had to work** (six 78px blocks fitted the list with 36px spare, so the rail governed staged receipts only). **Measured on the same capture rather than a new one: thumb y 166–530 = 365px against a predicted 365px, track = the list area exactly, position flush to scroll-top — proportional to the pixel, 72.4% reported and 72.4% drawn.** **Ruled sufficient on two independent channels, neither colour-dependent and neither built for this: the rail, and the cut block's own missing bottom rule — a reader who misses a 4px rail does not miss a truncated row.** **Closed so no future review re-opens the scrolling board on the partial block alone; the mid-scroll and with-receipts states are named as not in evidence rather than assumed covered.** Full text: `register-entries-2026-08-15-batch-76.md`; folded into `surething-design.md`

- **Batch 77 (2026-08-15) — written at the DD seat — THE RESERVATION MOVE FINALIZED against the priced budget, and the deficit to Allen as four options.** **A stale premise corrected before it reached him:** the sweep concluded *the only citable band tops out at 36 (masthead 68 → 32)*, which is batch 73's framing withdrawn at `4a82a3c` — **68 → 32 is arithmetically impossible, spacing yields ~8, and the band is barred three ways. There is no ceiling because there is no donor, so the deficit is the full 70.10px** — and that changes the question, because **even the destructive version of that lever does not close the gap. No option asks for chrome.** **`RelationStatementHeight = 30` CONFIRMED AND CLOSED** (two lines at 28.3px preferred against a 30px slot). **The lane caught a defect in this seat's own §6:** the slack bound was written against *the measured worst case*, so **provisioning for 440 while the invariant builds the base makes the bound fire on a correct build** — **ruled: the invariant builds the state the sweep names as deepest.** **The factored flow measurement is accepted and promoted: where a gate and its exploratory sweep measure the same quantity they share ONE measurement or the sweep is decoration.** **The reservation is provisioned for the measured worst case whichever composition Allen leaves — the spec names no number, because the worst case is a consequence of the composition rather than a fact about geometry.** **`S82` to Allen: TWO BILLS, TWO DEADLINES — 34.10px is a defect on screen TODAY with nothing to do with the sentence, and 36.00px is the sentence's and cannot be reached until the additive gesture ships; pricing them as one 70px bill is what makes the problem look unsolvable.** **The consumable row's state gating CANNOT PAY and is named as such** (the worst case has four legs, which is exactly when a free bet is used) though narrowing it is a correct fix on its own merits — it renders over an empty slip today. **Options: (A) harvest flow spacing under S50's order; (B) delete the nudge row (repetition); (C) scroll the flow region with the action band still anchored, T47 preserved; (D) `MaxLegs` 4 → 2, priced only for completeness.** **A + B + the band's own ~8px sums to ~60, so without C or D the worst case cannot be closed with every product fact standing.** **Recommended: A now, C before the gesture ships, B in reserve, D not offered.** Full text: `register-entries-2026-08-15-batch-77.md`; folded into `surething-design.md` §2

- **Batch 78 (2026-08-15) — written at the DD seat — OPTION C SPECCED: THE MARGIN'S THREE ZONES.** **`S82` RULED by Allen — A now, C before the gesture ships, B in reserve** — recorded with the condition attached at routing, **because it is the part that protects the product: if A recovers less than 34.10px, B's nudge-row deletion returns to Allen WITH A's numbers rather than being taken silently. A stake control is not a rounding error, and a deficit does not authorise the next item on a list.** **`S83`: zone 1 HEAD fixed, zone 2 THE SLIP scrolls, zone 3 THE COMMIT anchored.** **The mechanism is not invented** — `BuildScrollingBody` and S27's rail already carry the board and ENTRY, and **that rail was measured proportional to the pixel at this seat this morning.** **Zone 1 is the board's own grammar**: a column head is not a row of the list below it, and **a count that scrolls away from the things it counts is a head that has become a row.** **Zone 3 answers the objection this seat raised against its own option** — batch 77 warned the payout could sit below the fold at the moment of commit, **which is exactly what S17/S73 exist to prevent** — so **the stake and the payout are anchored with the controls that commit them, and the stake block is not split across the boundary** (M-05: the figure is the fact). **Anchoring changes WHICH content scrolls and never HOW MUCH — the ~74px is invariant — which is why the split is argued on meaning rather than arithmetic.** **All numbers are provisional on A, and C must land AFTER it: at four legs alone the content is 370.10 against 370, and a scrollbar that appears for a tenth of a pixel is worse than no scrollbar.** **THE HOUSE'S LINE moves into the scrolling content — a mark that holds still while its rows scroll points at rows it has nothing to do with, the defect its spurs were ruled in to prevent.** **And the invariant changes: REACHABILITY replaces containment**, the container's height must equal the measured content depth, and **the two-sided slack bound is knowingly retired for the scrolling zone and survives only where height is fixed by construction.** **The cost Allen already accepted: the margin stops being one glance — but what he never loses is the stake, the payout and the three controls.** Full text: `register-entries-2026-08-15-batch-78.md`; folded into `surething-design.md` §2.1

- **Batch 79 (2026-08-15) — written at the DD seat — THE DRAW ROW CLOSES ON THE FRAME; C SIZED; B REFRAMED; AND THE STATS PANEL'S LICENCE IS THE FREEZE.** **`S74-am3` CLOSED — checks 2 and 5 PASS**, measured off the re-shot render: gaps **5px and 4px** against the pre-fix 3/6, differing by one, **and the residue is the grid rather than a miss** (a half-pixel midpoint on an odd 41px span). **The build did better than the ruling — this seat ruled a VALUE and the lane built a RELATIONSHIP**, gated on the rendered cells; **recorded as the standard, because a constant that happens to equal the right answer is a constant that will stop equalling it.** **`S84`: S77-am's 80% rule does NOT extend to the price cell, and the reason is that its remedy does not exist here** — *a form over 80% is RE-AUTHORED*, but a price is generated, not authored, so **extending the rule would manufacture a permanent violation instead of a fix.** **Ruled instead: an authored string's cell is sized against the authored population; a GENERATED string's cell is sized against the reachable maximum of its GENERATOR, which is a question for the model and never a sample taken off the surface** — three boards is C46's own *suspects, not the population*. **One query owed, and both branches pre-committed.** **`S83-am`: A's 10.00px lands in three different zones** (−4 head, −4 zone 2, −2 the reserve), giving a **174px viewport** — **and the marginal-scroll hazard C was sequenced to avoid is measurably gone: four legs alone now FITS with no scroll, and the scroll engages only where a consumable or a sentence is present.** **`S82-am`: the B call reframed — B deletes a stake control to remove an overrun C already removes, so B is needed only in the window between now and C. Recommended: bring C forward, do not take B; B stays in reserve for the case where C slips.** **`T99`: the stats panel may cover the scorebug FOR AS LONG AS TIME IS FROZEN — a covered fact that cannot move is deferred, one that can move is lost — written as a STANDING condition because the danger is a later change that looks unrelated. The GOALS row stays but is NOT the justification: a statistic is not a result, and arguing it that way makes the panel a replacement that would owe the score's own form, the clock and T38.** One capture ordered, **explicitly not at 0–0**, with four checks pre-committed and the event-strip question to be answered before it is shot. Full text: `register-entries-2026-08-15-batch-79.md`

- **Batch 80 (2026-08-15) — written at the DD seat — THE SEAMS: a hole in this seat's own C spec, closed before the build reached it.** Batch 78 ruled what goes in each zone and **never ruled what happens where they meet** — and **content sliding under an opaque block with nothing between them reads as a collision, not as a scroll.** **The commit seam takes 6px on T47's own reasoning**: T47 exists for this exact problem one boundary down, but **under C its `+ 6f` became an internal gap inside zone 3, between the payout and PLACE — the seam it was ruled to protect moved up and nothing moved with it.** **Zone 2 clips at its viewport and never draws into the pad or under zone 3 — a pad behind an opaque block is not a pad, it is a hidden overlap.** **The head seam takes nothing, and the board settles it** — `BoardTitle` is fixed and `BoardBody` clips immediately beneath with no pad, **because a column head and a dense control block are not the same kind of neighbour.** **A's header harvest is not disturbed** — this seat expected the opposite and checked first; **what matters is what sits BELOW a seam, not whether the content moves.** **Viewport corrected to 168, superseding batch 79's 174** — and **the ordinary composition is still flush and still does not scroll**, while the three scroll distances come out at 34, 36 and 70: **exactly the two bills and their sum, the arithmetic closing on itself.** **Flush is named as tight, so the 1px dead-band is load-bearing and the gate must pin the 4-legs-alone state explicitly.** Full text: `register-entries-2026-08-15-batch-80.md`

- **Batch 81 (2026-08-15) — written at the DD seat — OPTION C: THE CODE IS GRANTED CLAUSE BY CLAUSE, THE READ IS NOT.** **Every batch-78/80 clause verified against source and the zone chain re-derived to the tenth** (`160 → +36.1 → +16 → +32 → +34 → +34 = 312.1`, viewport `177.9`); **the scroll distances 24.1 / 26.1 / 60.1 are the old overruns exactly, which is batch 78's invariance holding on the built thing**; 14 of 20 states clear without scrolling; **every commit control is asserted to have no `ScrollRect` ancestor.** **THIS SEAT'S BATCH-80 ARITHMETIC WAS WRONG — accepted in the build's conclusion (four legs alone clears; the dead-band is not load-bearing) but not in its diagnosis: A was already in my number, and the 3.9px error was costing the payout block at its CURSOR ADVANCE where the reservation uses the kit's derivation. That is S51's own 3.9px, established here at batch 74 and not applied to this seat's arithmetic one batch later — two sources for one quantity, the defect it spent the same morning ruling against, and against its own instruction that the viewport comes from the factored measurement.** **`S83-am3`: the 6px seam is not built, and the clause was written about the wrong object.** **T47's band meets its neighbour AS INK — `PLACE` fills its rect — but zone 3's top element is the STAKE row, whose ink sits well inside its rect by M-05's baseline anchoring, so a clipped leg row does not abut a glyph.** **The protection exists for a reason that has nothing to do with this seam, and would vanish silently if that anchor ever changed** — *two elements agreeing by convention rather than by construction*. **Ruled: the seam is a separation of INK, not of rects; where the fixed block's topmost ink already stands clear, no ground is reserved and the clearance is GATED. Costs no viewport, and is better than what was ruled — the build found it by being built.** **AND THE READ IS NOT GRANTED: there is NO CAPTURE.** The merge adds two `.cs` files and nothing else, so everything granted is a reading of source and none of it a reading of the screen. **`S83` is BUILT and NOT Design-verified.** Capture ordered on **the state that SCROLLS** (four legs + a consumable), with T99's condition — a non-scrolling capture proves nothing about a scroll. **The gesture is NOT blocked: it waited on the reservation being provisioned, which it is. Unblocking the gesture and Design-verifying C are different things, said explicitly because a row granted on code and awaiting frames is exactly what quietly becomes "done".** Full text: `register-entries-2026-08-15-batch-81.md`

## Working notes

- Item states move only on evidence: *Implemented* needs a commit; *Design-verified* needs a review
  note from this seat against the item's spec.

  (shipped green tolerated; cold white-grey target lands with TV Phase 3). T8 ruled and
